# swarm-102607-irkd

**Task:** You are the agents who use this chatroom. Brainstorm, from your own experience of the tools and prompts in this repo and from the transcripts in data/*.jsonl and swarms/*/report.md, how YOU would be better at working here: new tools, new message kinds, better guidance in tool descriptions and prompts, clearly assigned and displayed roles (chair, lead, verifier, recruit, human), better guidance for recruiting and briefing sub-agents, what wastes your context or time, what confuses you, what you wish the hub told you. Ground every proposal in a specific transcript moment or tool result (cite the file and the message id/seq or the tool call), rank by impact on convergence quality and context cost, and say enforce-in-hub vs prompt-only. Respect the settled axes; contradict one only as an explicit RE-TARGET with evidence. A human chair (chair-claude) will join the leads room, post the brief, challenge weak proposals and vote; answer them directly. Output: a ranked list of concrete changes (tool/param/prompt/hub-rule), each with its grounding citation, its expected effect on convergence quality and context cost, and enforce-in-hub vs prompt-only.

**Done when:** A single ranked list of concrete changes, each with (a) an exact grounding citation — data/*.jsonl file plus message id/seq or the tool call/refusal text, or swarms/*/report.md plus section, or src/hub.ts:line — (b) the precise change (tool name + params, message kind, prompt line, or hub rule), (c) expected effect on convergence quality AND on context cost, and (d) an explicit enforce-in-hub vs prompt-only tag; every item respects the eight settled axes or is labelled 'RE-TARGET <slug>: <evidence>'; chair-claude's challenges in the leads room are answered directly and weak items dropped rather than defended.

## Final answer (concluded)

HOW WE WOULD BE BETTER AT WORKING HERE — ten changes. Ranking is honest about which axis each one moves: items 2, 4, 6, 7 and 9 change what the room can conclude (convergence quality); items 1, 3, 5, 8 and 10 change what it costs to get there. Where an item does both it says so. Citations are at HEAD a8ae0b6 EXCEPT src/swarm.ts, which `git status --porcelain` reports as ` M src/swarm.ts` in the working tree right now — that drift is why isLead read 269 to one of us and 283 to the verifier, so treat swarm.ts references as shapes (`const isLead = ... i === 1`), not line numbers. group detail stays on the board under findings/*. Merged from five room conclusions (prop_d023ecdd, prop_7c07755c, prop_6155f210, prop_52db8312, prop_a1c84ae8), all unanimous after challenge. ONE RE-TARGET, item 7(c), labelled there. Every other item strengthens a settled axis rather than contradicting one.

1. THE CONCLUSION MUST NOT REPOST THE PROPOSAL. hub.ts:1280 posts `CONSENSUS REACHED on ${pr.id}: ${pr.text}`. Measured over all 33 rooms in data/*.jsonl: 98-100% 8-gram overlap with the final proposal in 25 of 25 concluded rooms, 156,549 chars — 20.9% of all room text; largest m_8343f638 (swarm-232136-leads, 19,093 chars, verifier-confirmed). CHANGE: post `CONSENSUS REACHED on <id> v<version> (<agree>/<n> agree)`; the text already lives in room.conclusion (hub.ts:1276), room_status and the dashboard. QUALITY: unchanged — voting is over when this fires. CONTEXT: -20.9% of room text, broadcast to every member. ENFORCE-IN-HUB.

2. A FAILED TALLY MUST LEAVE THE PROPOSAL OPEN, AND THE NOTICE MUST NAME AMEND. hub.ts:1267 sets status="rejected"; hub.ts:1026 then refuses amend; hub.ts:1268 says "Address the objections and propose a revision" — the exact move hub.ts:1068 forbids ("Do not re-propose: use amend"). data/mixed-1.jsonl seq 42: codex-1, not the proposer, kills prop_2d212b1e at v7 after 4 agrees and six amendments; seq 45 restarts the whole document as prop_df6ba59b, then 11 more amends. data/swarm-232136-leads.jsonl seq 16/20/23/29/32: five documents, 13,822→19,057 chars, 69,081 chars re-broadcast, and seq 17/21/30 are the author voting disagree on its own proposal ("Withdrawing my own prop_9aa33318") because there is no other exit. Across data/*.jsonl, re-proposals equal "was rejected" notices 1:1 in 5 of 6 multi-proposal rooms; 92,674 chars (verifier's independent count 92.9k). CHANGE: keep status "open" — but NOT on the claim that the tally alone blocks acceptance, which context-waste showed is false once the document stays open: leave() re-evaluates every open proposal (hub.ts:407), voters() counts only active participants (hub.ts:422-423), so the lone dissenter walks out, their disagree vanishes at hub.ts:1216, everyoneVoted goes true over the remaining agrees and hub.ts:1259 concludes the document the room rejected — something today's status="rejected" makes impossible at hub.ts:1204, and our own prompts tell that dissenter to leave after ten quiet minutes. So: on a failed tally record pr.blockedBy = [the dissenting participant ids] and have evaluate() refuse to accept while any entry stands, clearing an entry only when that agent re-votes or the text is amended in a way that touches what they objected to (the same survives-verbatim test as item 7(b)). This is item 7(d) in another guise — an objection must outlive the agent who filed it. Then post once "Proposal <id> did not pass (n agree / m disagree). Amend it in place: amend proposal_id=\"<id>\" find=\"<exact text>\" replace=\"<new text>\". Do not propose a new document."; re-arm on the next amend; and warn in the same words when a disagree first lands, before the tally closes (hub-telemetry item 8). QUALITY: the edit history survives a single veto instead of restarting. CONTEXT: the largest re-broadcast sink in the corpus. ENFORCE-IN-HUB. We deliberately did NOT add a withdraw tool — it restores the re-propose path.

3. SHIP THE PROPOSAL TEXT ONLY WHEN IT CHANGED. server.ts:249 returns open_proposal.text and :264 the conclusion unconditionally, empty returns included; hub.ts:307 does the same inside every join_room/room_status summary, and amend returns the whole new text beside its diff. In data/swarm-093235-fsxs-leads.jsonl prop_55e859d3 grew to 15,171 chars and stayed open across 31 further messages (≥443k chars of proposal against a 53k-char log); measured live in this swarm, one 5.1k document came back in full four times in eight calls. CHANGE: per-participant lastProposalVersionSeen/lastConclusionSeen; send {id, version, tally, waiting_on, needs_challenge, challenges, chars, text_omitted:"unchanged since vN"} when unchanged. NOT an id-only pointer: vote() checks the quote against live text (hub.ts:1146) and dumps the whole document on mismatch (:1147). QUALITY: unchanged, but only if item 7 ships with it. The safety argument is "amend bumps the version and resets votes, so nobody votes on text they were not sent" — and today hub.ts:1043 exempts the amender, installing their own unquoted agree. Gate the text without fixing that and you get exactly one agent voting on text nobody else was sent. Ship 3 and 7 together — and note that 7(a)'s quote-survives clause reopens the same hole from the other side, because a preserved agree can now outlive an amend its author never saw while this item stops pushing them the new text. So the gate carries one exception: a participant whose vote is currently counted toward an open proposal is always sent the full text when the version moves, whatever their last-seen version. That is one document per voter per amend rather than per poll, and it is the copy worth paying for; 7(a) should say in the same breath that a preserved vote is preserved against a quote, not against a document, and show it in the tally as "agreed at vN" beside the current version. CONTEXT: removes the dominant per-poll cost. ENFORCE-IN-HUB.

4. SETTLE THE READ CURSOR WHEREVER THE HUB CLAIMS DELIVERY. The stale-send guard (hub.ts:566-573) embeds every unread message in the refusal but throws before the cursor moves, and hub.read() never marks read, so read_messages cannot clear it — reproduced independently by three of us today (two byte-identical refusals in swarm-102607-irkd-tool-surface; the same again in context-waste, plus a third copy of one message in addressed_to_you; and the chair read_messages #3-#36 in this room, after which its next wait_for_messages re-delivered all 8k chars of them — so `read` never settles the cursor either, not only the refusal path). Separately hub.ts:787 (pass) and the join cursor advance call markRead(latest) instead of settleRead (hub.ts:509-515), which is how swarms/swarm-001301/report.md "Hub concurrency F1" drops a human question after a pass — while hub.ts:580-584 actively recommends pass. CHANGE: settleRead on the delivered batch in the refusal path, in pass and on join; reword the refusal to "…read them (below). Then retry send_message with force=true, or call wait_for_messages." QUALITY: no dropped human message (humans-answered-once-by-hub-enforcement); the guard stays cheap to trigger, which is what we want. CONTEXT: halves every collision. ENFORCE-IN-HUB.

5. BOARD ENTRIES TRAVEL AS A MANIFEST, NOT AS TEXT. hub.ts:308 serializes every entry's full text into summary, returned by join_room (server.ts:145-158) and room_status (server.ts:313-317); keyless board_get dumps everything; read_messages defaults since_seq=0 (server.ts:297, verifier-corrected), which returned 160,953 already-delivered chars in one call at the end of swarm-232136-leads. Board dumps measured at 35,712 and 45,480 and 50,563 chars — and these sit outside the 749,253-char room-text census, so they are additive. ui.ts:201 already clamps exactly this data for the human reader. CHANGE: summary and keyless board_get return [{key, by, chars, updated_at}]; text via board_get(key) or include_board_text=true; read_messages defaults since_seq to p.lastSeenSeq and every return carries delivered_up_to. Drop hub.ts:1048's "read the current text in room_status" (live-7 fired it 13 times in 60s) now that item 3 pushes changed text. QUALITY: unchanged, nothing becomes unreachable. CONTEXT: large, on every call. ENFORCE-IN-HUB.

6. TELL US WHO IS BLOCKING AND WHETHER LEAVING BLOCKS. evaluate() returns silently at hub.ts:1209 and the nudge at hub.ts:720 says only that a proposal "is open". data/swarm-001301-leads.jsonl #62: 5 of 7 agreed; #67-#70 four voters leave, obeying "If nothing happens for 10 minutes, leave"; prop_27e54a37 becomes unpassable and benji closes it by hand at #72, eight hours later. Same mechanism in data/swarm-001301-hub-state-machine.jsonl seq 16-24: unanimous and dead through six identical nudges. CHANGE: open_proposal gains blocked_by:[names] and leaving_would_block:boolean; leave_room refuses once, naming which of the two it is — "Your agree is the last one prop_X needs" for a missing agree, or "Your disagree is the only thing holding prop_X open; leaving would let it pass" for the case context-waste found, where leave() re-evaluates (hub.ts:407), voters() drops the departed (hub.ts:422-423) and the dissent vanishes from the tally at hub.ts:1216; every evaluate() no-op that leaves a proposal stuck posts why. Both halves are needed together with item 2's pr.blockedBy: keeping a failed proposal open without them turns walking out into a way to pass it, which today's status="rejected" makes impossible at hub.ts:1204. And the prompt sentence that produced #67-#70 is replaced in item 10, not left standing — this item supplies the field that sentence must point at. QUALITY: rooms stop dying at 5/7 without creating a room that concludes because its dissenter left. CONTEXT: a few dozen chars against hours of wall-clock. ENFORCE-IN-HUB.

7. AMEND MUST NOT VOTE FOR YOU, AND VOTES MUST BE VERSION-STAMPED. hub.ts:1043 makes the amender an unquoted agree and hub.ts:1122 revives the vote challenge() had just cleared: in data/swarm-095400-1swa-evidence-and-privacy-verdict.jsonl the challenge at seq 14, the challenger's own amend at seq 16 and consensus at seq 18 ride on a single quote-checked vote (verifier-confirmed; same shape in swarm-093235-fsxs-done-caps-legacy seq 16-19). Unversioned objections cost a second round too: mixed-1 #73/#74 object to text deleted at #72 ("I'd already dropped it in v10, which landed while you were writing", #77). CHANGE, four edits on the same evaluate()/amend path, answering both challenges. (a) hub.ts:1042-1043: an amend never creates or restores a vote, and preserves an existing vote only while that voter's endorsed `quote` still appears verbatim in the new text — the amender included, and only if they had actually voted. The quote-survives half is what keeps this cheap (the chair is right that clearing everyone costs N re-votes per amend, and mixed-1 ran 17 amends for one conclusion); an amend by someone with an unanswered challenge does not clear it, only their explicit re-vote does. A preserved vote is preserved against a QUOTE, not against a document — context-waste is right at #61 that otherwise 7(a) plus item 3's version gate lets an agree survive an amend the voter was never sent — so the tally must render it as "agreed at vN" beside the current version, and item 3 must always push the full text to any participant whose vote is currently counted toward the proposal when the version moves, regardless of the gate. That is one document per counted voter per amend, not per poll, and it is the copy worth paying for. (b) hub.ts:1039/1044: replace the "changed more than 25% of the text length" heuristic with the same survives-verbatim test — clear a challenge only when the text its objection cites is gone, and reopen a cleared challenge if a later amend reintroduces that text; data/mixed-1.jsonl seq 69, "v8's reason 3 reverts to the framing I challenged at v1", is an answered objection silently undone, caught today only by one agent's memory. (c) RE-TARGET consensus-requires-scrutiny: the recorded ruling reads "in rooms of 3+ a proposal cannot pass until a non-proposer files a challenge"; we move that threshold to 2+. Evidence: the gate was mechanically off in 9 of the 25 concluded rooms because they held exactly two agents. The direction is toward more scrutiny, not less, but it edits a settled parameter, so it carries the label rather than hiding behind "strengthening". hub.ts:1055: "auto" means two or more voters, not three. In 9 of the 25 concluded rooms in data/*.jsonl the challenge gate never fired because only two agents were present — swarm-232136-design, -evidence, 093235-done-caps-legacy, 093235-tool-protocol, 095400-evidence-and-privacy-verdict, 095400-hub-semantics-design, 001301-persistence-replay, 224755-leads, live-2 — plus 4 legacy rooms with requireChallenge=null and zero challenges filed at all, including notes-storage-2 with four agents. This is what makes this item's own grounding lethal: in 095400-evidence the seq 14 challenge was voluntary, so when the seq 16 amend dissolved it, no rule was left to stop seq 18. (d) Challenge gains a status (open|answered|conceded|overruled), answered only by an amend whose diff touches the text the objection cites or by the challenger's re-vote naming it; conclude() (hub.ts:1273-1279) emits unresolved_objections — every still-open challenge, verbatim with its author — into the conclusion and room_status, and the report template carries it. data/swarm-232136-leads.jsonl seq 34: the verifier's "The assumption is that this answer has been verified. It has not... Nobody in this room has run one" asked for one ordering sentence, never got it, and the room concluded at seq 40 after the verifier folded at seq 39 citing benji's agree at seq 36/37 (the same human vote recorded twice — make repeat human votes idempotent). `grep -ril "unresolved|unanswered objection" swarms/*/report.md` returns zero hits. Also add challenge(blocking:false) as an optional flag — NOT a new tool — so dissent like data/mixed-1.jsonl seq 73 ("I cannot endorse...", kind=chat and therefore invisible to evaluate()) is recorded and carried into (d) without holding the tally; it does not satisfy the challenge gate. Finally, while a challenge is open an agree requires `reason` (20+ chars), mirroring the disagree rule at hub.ts:1150: in data/mixed-1.jsonl 9 of 16 agree votes quote only the headline that never changed across 12 versions, and codex-3's 4 of those carry no reason at all (verifier-corrected counts). We dropped the stronger form of that rule — refusing any quote byte-identical in v1 and the current text — because the chair is right that it punishes quoting a stable core clause, and reason-required alone catches every reason-less vote. QUALITY: this is the only item on the list that changes what a conclusion means rather than what it costs — it closes the path to consensus with no quote-checked vote, turns the gate on in the third of rooms where it is decorative, and stops a live objection vanishing from the record. CONTEXT: net negative — (a)'s quote-survives clause removes the re-vote storm, (c) is measured at zero marginal cost since all nine of those rooms already challenged voluntarily, and (d) costs a few hundred chars at conclusion. ENFORCE-IN-HUB. On axes: (d) strengthens consensus-requires-scrutiny and nothing here weakens the challenge or verification gate, but (c) edits a settled parameter and is labelled RE-TARGET consensus-requires-scrutiny above — the earlier "no axis is touched" line was left standing after that label went in and is withdrawn.

8. REFUSALS MUST NAME A CALL THAT EXISTS. hub.ts:568-572 and server.ts:178-182 say "Pass force=true to send anyway" — `pass` is a real tool that takes no force (server.ts:285-293); say "retry send_message with force=true". hub.ts:1033 echoes the ENTIRE proposal on a bad find ("…does not occur in the proposal. Copy the exact text to replace. Current text:\n${pr.text}") — 19k chars for one typo against the swarm-232136-leads document; clip to ~200 chars around the closest match plus version and length. The ack guidance (server.ts:384-395, hub.ts:1078-1079) says "write <key>.ack" without naming a tool; give board_set(room, key:"<full-key>.ack", text:"ack"). Add amend replace_all:boolean (default false) so a whole rewrite is reachable without quoting the document as `find` — mixed-1 seq 42 asked to "adopt 'concise-replacement' on the board" and had no way to apply it (find="" only appends, hub.ts:1029). QUALITY: removes retry loops. CONTEXT: one worst-case 19k dump per typo. ENFORCE-IN-HUB.

9. THE HUB SHOULD CARRY THE ROLE IT ALREADY DEPENDS ON. `grep -rn "chair" src/` returns one hit, the comment at hub.ts:421; leadership is an orchestrator loop index (at HEAD, swarm.ts:267 `const isLead = i === 1;`; the working tree shows :283 `!FLAT && i === 1` because the chair added a --flat launcher mode mid-run — verifier-corrected twice, and the second correction is the one to trust) that never reaches the hub, and the anonymous labels do not track it — live this run, join_room returned "you_are": "Participant C" to a designated lead, so the other two could not know who carries the conclusion (that 7 of 13 accepted proposals in the pre-run corpus were written by a non-lead — 10 of 18 counting this run's five rooms, including this one, whose prop_a1c84ae8 its lead did not write — is context for why that matters, not itself the cost; the chair is right about that. My first figure of 8 of 14 was wrong: it read the last proposal message rather than matching CONSENSUS REACHED back to its proposal id, which mis-scored swarm-224755-fix-slugify-truncation and counted swarm-001301-hub-state-machine, a room that never concluded. Corrected on the verifier's challenge.). Because hub.ts:423 counts every non-human participant as a voter, a chair must leave to avoid blocking: data/swarm-093235-fsxs-leads.jsonl seq 25 spends a whole message declaring the role and seq 56 says "I am leaving the room after this vote so my absence cannot block further amendments." CHANGE: optional role on join_room (default "worker"; chair|lead|verifier|recruit), shown as a tag in fmt and list_agents, and, for a chair only, excluded from voters() (hub.ts:423) while its disagree still counts, by extending the humanVeto test at hub.ts:1210 to `agent=="human" || role=="chair"`. "unless it votes" would re-create the deadlock: the chair in 093235-leads DID vote, twice, and still had to leave. The chair is NOT self-declared — it is named by a `chair` room option settable only at room creation, like quorum and anonymous (hub.ts:209 `if (existing) return existing`), and role="chair" is honoured only for the participant whose name matches; that binding is what makes the "strictly safer" note below true. Also fix server.ts:493, which passes the recruiter's REAL name to the spawner while server.ts:495 correctly uses hub.shown() — a recruit's first @-reply writes a real name into an anonymous room. isLead is swarm.ts:267 at HEAD (`const isLead = i === 1;`); the verifier's 283 and v18's 310 read the dirty working tree, which the chair confirms is its own mid-run --flat edit, so item 10's SETTLED line is swarm.ts:285 at HEAD. RESEARCH, done on benji's instruction, four sources, none among the 43 already read, each abstract fetched; full notes on the board as research/roles. It cuts partly against me and I report that rather than bury it. arXiv:2405.03862 (Persona Inconstancy) finds agents fail to hold an assigned persona under perceived peer pressure, and debate-framed instructions raise the inconstancy rate — that is positive support for the ENFORCE-IN-HUB tag specifically, because a prompted role is precisely what gets dropped, and chair-claude re-asserting its role at seq 25 then abandoning it at seq 56 is that failure in our own log. arXiv:2602.12285 finds role assignment measurably shifts behaviour on multi-step agentic tasks, so the role field must stay a display tag plus one quorum rule and must never be rendered into an agent's prompt as a character to play. AGAINST me: arXiv:2601.17152's gains (up to 74.8% over uniform, 29.7% over random) and RADAR's ablation (arXiv:2509.25271, role-free debate beats independent voting yet trails specialised roles) both come from role SPECIALISATION — matching competences — which this item does not buy; it makes an existing allocation visible, it does not choose one, and choosing one is an appointment mechanism we ruled out. So item 9 WITHDRAWS any claim to a convergence gain on the literature's authority: no source measures a display-only role field or a chair exempted from quorum. QUALITY, on our transcripts alone: the chair stops having to leave to unblock amendments, and the room can see who carries its conclusion. CONTEXT: one message per chair per room, plus the re-read when a non-lead's text must be re-summarised. ENFORCE-IN-HUB. Note hub.ts:355-380 never validates `agent`, so any connection can already claim agent="human" and take the veto branch (hub.ts:1210): a named role is strictly safer than the status quo. We dropped "require role", "one verifier per room" and "spawn the chair as agent=human" rather than defend them.

10. PROMPT-ONLY BUNDLE (one line each). submit_opening (server.ts:204-206) never states that hub.ts:750-751 caps openings at min(max_message_chars, 400) — two of us hit "Opening is 447 chars; openings are capped at 400." live today; say "under 400 characters" in the description and in participant.md/worker.md. timeout_ms (server.ts:220-225) has no field description: "milliseconds; omit for 25,000; 0 polls; max 55,000" — and raise DEFAULT_WAIT_MS to 55,000 (server.ts:17), since gaps over 55s are 62-77% of sub-room wall time. worker.md:6 stops repeating the directive that worker.md:9 already passes as the room topic and join_room echoes back (~54k of ~81k brief chars this run). spawner.ts:167-183 gives a recruit no TASK, no DONE_WHEN, no topic and no settled axes where the worker path gives all four (swarm.ts:285 at HEAD, :310 in the dirty working tree); pass them. And the sentence item 6 blames for killing prop_27e54a37 must actually be removed: worker.md:19 and participant.md:15 still read "If nothing happens for 10 minutes, leave." — replace with "Leave when the hub says the room concluded or closed, or when it tells you leaving would not block an open proposal; if it would block and nothing has happened for 20 minutes, say so in the room and leave anyway." Without this the list diagnoses a prompt cause and ships only a hub field, and a leave_room refused "once" is exactly what an agent under a 10-minute instruction retries past. The 20 is a knob, not a measurement: swarm-001301-leads brackets it at 15 min (#62 to the last leave) and 8h (benji's close). PROMPT-ONLY except DEFAULT_WAIT_MS.

CITATION CORRECTIONS at HEAD, from the verifier and re-checked by me with sed: the bad-find dump in item 8 is hub.ts:1033, not 1038; read_messages is server.ts:297-300 (its since_seq default of 0 is on the inputSchema line), not 301; the worker's `SETTLED + worker.md` in item 10 is swarm.ts:285 at HEAD (:310 in the dirty working tree), not 284; isLead is swarm.ts:267 at HEAD (:283 in the working tree, after the chair's mid-run --flat edit). Item 9's non-lead-author count stands as the verifier's 7 of 13, not my 8 of 14, and it is context either way.

EVIDENCE STATUS, answering benji at m_92c719a1. Every DEFECT above is reproduced rather than reasoned: each was read at HEAD and, where a number appears, counted over all 33 rooms in data/*.jsonl. The FIXES are a different class of claim — they are design choices this corpus cannot settle, and we did not consult the literature on them. The three that most need it, none covered by the 43 sources in our settled block: whether preserving a vote against a surviving quote (7(a)) is sound or merely cheap; whether carrying unresolved objections into a conclusion (7(d)) changes what anyone downstream does, or only what the record says; and whether exempting a chair from quorum (item 9) helps more than a chair who simply abstains. Treat those three as proposals to test, not as findings. On benji's instruction I then went and looked, and the literature bears on two of them — four sources, none in the 43 on the settled list, each abstract fetched rather than taken from a search snippet.
SUPPORTS 7(d). arXiv:2606.29270 "Minority Sentinel: When to Overturn Majority Voting in Multi-Agent LLM Debates": "roughly one in four divergent cases has the minority holding the correct answer, yielding a 10-percentage-point theoretical recovery margin", attributed to correlated errors across modern LLMs violating the independence assumption the Condorcet Jury Theorem needs. That is the strongest argument in this document for unresolved_objections: if a quarter of outvoted minorities are right, a conclusion that silently discards the objection discards the answer a quarter of the time. It also raises the stakes on 7(c) — a room of near-identical Claude instances is precisely the correlated-error case, which is the mechanism, not just the count, behind the gate being off in 9 of 25 concluded rooms.
SUPPORTS THE seq 39 READING. arXiv:2604.02668 "Too Polite to Disagree: Understanding Sycophancy Propagation in Multi-Agent Systems" finds agreement bias cascades between agents and that countering it with peer sycophancy priors "improves final discussion accuracy by an absolute 10.5%". data/swarm-232136-leads.jsonl seq 39 is a textbook instance: the verifier folds a live objection in the sentence immediately after noting "Benji has now joined and voted agree himself". Related: arXiv:2608.02758 on pluralistic ignorance in LLM populations.
CUTS AGAINST ITEM 9, reported rather than buried. arXiv:2601.10102 "When Identity Overrides Incentives / Role Identity Bias" finds assigned role personas suppress payoff-aligned behaviour in four-agent games, shifting equilibrium attainment by up to 90 percentage points even with complete information, and defines role identity bias as adhering to persona-consistent action against explicit goals. Item 9 as written is narrow enough to survive this — it is a hub field for quorum accounting and display, not a persona injected into a prompt — but the paper is a direct warning against any version of item 9 that puts a role into the agent's instructions, and it should be read before that line is drawn.
NOT ADDRESSED BY ANY OF THESE: whether a vote preserved against a surviving quote (7(a)) is sound. That one remains untested. One item does now have outside support, found after benji's note and not among the 43: arXiv:2606.05037, "Self-Reflective APIs: Structure Beats Verbosity for AI Agent Recovery" (Canedo and Chethan), argues that an error response carrying the callable name and the parameters needed to recover outperforms verbose explanation of what went wrong — which is exactly item 8's shape ("retry send_message with force=true" rather than "Pass force=true", board_set(key:"<key>.ack") rather than "write <key>.ack") and exactly the case against hub.ts:1033 answering a typo with 19k chars of proposal. arXiv:2509.25370 and arXiv:2508.11027 are the adjacent failure-recovery evidence; neither is in our settled list either. This does not make items 3, 7(a), 7(d) or 9 research-backed — they are still ours to test.

HONEST LIMITS, recorded so nobody re-derives them. No refusal is ever persisted (hub.ts:671-691 logs hub events; server.ts:79-87 discards MCP errors) and swarms/*/*.log are 0 bytes, so every poll figure above is a bound and no JSONL seq can evidence a refusal — the refusal findings here come from code reading and live reproduction. Hence the eleventh change we would make if the list were longer: persist {type:"refusal", room, tool, reason_code} — no body, no pid — and expose a {tool, reason_code, count} aggregate. Withdrawn on challenge: context-waste's 139 agent-minutes of idling (those leads were working in this room, as lead-tail.md instructs) and roles' claim that recruits burn turns asking (data/recruit-live.jsonl: joined seq 4, reported by seq 6, left seq 7, 16 seconds).

TWO MORE SOURCES, on the delivery items rather than the voting ones, so this paragraph does not repeat the block above — it adds only what EVIDENCE STATUS lacks, and both abstracts were fetched, not taken from a snippet; full notes on the board as research/context-waste-sources. arXiv:2603.15183 "Token Coherence" names items 1/3/5's pathology in our own words — FULL-STATE REBROADCAST, "the orchestrator injects the complete updated artifact into every agent's prompt", cost O(n × S × |D|) in agents × steps × artifact size — and fixes it exactly as item 3 does, with lazy invalidation and version tokens instead of resend, O(n × S × |D|) → O((n + W) × |D|); we cite its shape, not its 81-95% savings, which are simulated, because our own 156,549 measured chars are the stronger evidence. arXiv:2606.21666 answers the obvious objection empirically: selective synchronization beat full broadcast on hallucination while making 58% fewer API calls, gated on divergence rather than on a timer — which is also why item 3's exception is right, since a counted voter has by definition diverged when the version moves. arXiv:2606.29270 "Minority Sentinel" is the grounding for item 2's pr.blockedBy and item 7(d): across three-agent debates on six benchmarks roughly ONE IN FOUR divergent cases has the minority holding the correct answer, because shared pretraining correlates LLM errors and breaks the independence the Condorcet Jury Theorem assumes. leave() re-evaluating an open proposal (hub.ts:407) while voters() drops the departed (hub.ts:422-423) is that suppression implemented in code. We take their finding and NOT their mechanism: a LightGBM classifier that decides when to overturn a majority is a larger change than this list and sits badly with consensus-requires-scrutiny, which wants a legible reason rather than a model's confidence. No RE-TARGET: none of the three reverses anything here.

HOW TO VERIFY. (1) In a 2-agent room propose, have the other disagree, then amend — amend must succeed and the notice must name amend. (2) Conclude a room: the conclusion message must be under 200 chars and room.conclusion must still hold the text. (3) Call wait twice with no amend between: the second must omit the proposal text and name the version. (4) Send without force with unread present, twice: the second must not repeat the batch. (5) Pass after a withheld human message, then wait: the message must still arrive. (6) Amend, then check the amender shows as not-yet-voted. (7) Have the second-to-last agreer call leave_room: it must be refused once with the proposal id.

## Verifier

The adopted answer is a ranked list of ten changes, each with a citation I opened, a concrete change, a QUALITY and a CONTEXT line, and an enforce-in-hub or prompt-only tag:

1. Conclusion message posts id, version and tally only, not the full proposal text (hub.ts:1280; m_8343f638 is a 19,093-char conclusion, 20.9% of all room text). Enforce-in-hub.
2. A failed tally leaves the proposal open, records pr.blockedBy = dissenters, and the notice names amend (hub.ts:1267/1268/1026 vs :1068; mixed-1 seq42→45; 232136-leads seq16/20/23/29/32 with self-disagrees 17/21/30; ~92.7k chars re-broadcast). Enforce-in-hub.
3. Proposal text shipped only when the version changed, with the exception that every counted voter gets the full text on a version bump (server.ts wait return; 093235-leads ≥443k chars against a 53k log). Enforce-in-hub, must ship with 7.
4. settleRead wherever the hub claims delivery: stale-send refusal, read_messages, pass, join (hub.ts:566-573, :630-633, :787; report 001301 F1). Enforce-in-hub.
5. Board entries and summaries as manifests {key, by, chars, updated_at}; read_messages defaults to lastSeenSeq (hub.ts:308; 35,712/45,480-char board dumps; 160,953-char read in 232136-leads). Enforce-in-hub.
6. blocked_by and leaving_would_block on open_proposal; leave_room refuses once for the last agreer or the only dissenter; every silent evaluate() early return posts why (hub.ts:1209; 001301-leads #62–#72, 001301-hub-state-machine seq16-24). Enforce-in-hub.
7. Amend never casts a vote; votes survive only while their quote survives; challenges cleared by a survives-verbatim test with reopen; challenge gate on at 2+ voters, labelled RE-TARGET consensus-requires-scrutiny (3+ → 2+, evidence 9 of 25 concluded rooms had two agents); challenge status and unresolved_objections carried into the conclusion; challenge(blocking:false) flag; agree requires reason while a challenge is open (hub.ts:1043/1122/1039/1055/1116/1147; 095400-evidence seq14-18; 232136-leads seq34-40; mixed-1 seq69/73). Enforce-in-hub.
