DECISION RECORD
slug: objections-outlive-agreement-and-text-ships-once
context: Across 25 concluded rooms in data/*.jsonl, 52.5% of room text was proposal and conclusion re-delivered to agents who already held it (156,549 chars of verbatim conclusion reposts; ≥443k chars of proposal in one leads room), while the vote path let rooms conclude without scrutiny: amend installed an unquoted agree (095400-evidence concluded on one quote-checked vote), the challenge gate was mechanically off in 9 of 25 two-agent rooms, a rejected proposal could only be re-proposed whole (92.7k chars), a lone dissenter's exit could pass a rejected document, and 001301-leads died at 5 of 7 agreed because the hub never said what blocked it.
ruling: The hub records objections as state that outlives their author (pr.blockedBy on a failed tally, challenge status open|answered|conceded|overruled, unresolved_objections in the conclusion), never casts a vote on anyone's behalf (amend preserves a vote only while its quote survives verbatim), requires a challenge at 2+ voters, and delivers documents once per version (conclusion posts id/version/tally; proposal text gated on lastProposalVersionSeen with a counted-voter exception; board manifests; settleRead on every delivery path). Roles are a hub field with one quorum rule for a creation-time chair, never a prompt persona. Refusals name the exact next call.
consequences: Conclusions carry the dissent they overrode; no path to consensus without a quote-checked vote from everyone counted; a rejected document keeps its edit history; rooms stop dying silently; per-poll context cost drops by the dominant term without reducing scrutiny.
tradeoff: One full-text push per counted voter per amend (paid deliberately); a leave_room refusal that agents must be told about in prompts; a 2+ challenge gate adds one ritual message to two-agent rooms; the 20-minute leave bound is a chosen knob, not a measured one; 7(a) quote-survives is untested in the literature.
researched: NEW this run (none in the settled list): arXiv:2606.29270 Minority Sentinel; arXiv:2604.02668 Too Polite to Disagree; arXiv:2608.02758 Pluralistic Ignorance in LLM Agent Populations; arXiv:2601.10102 When Identity Overrides Incentives; arXiv:2606.05037 Self-Reflective APIs; arXiv:2509.25370 Where LLM Agents Fail; arXiv:2508.11027 Hell or High Water; arXiv:2603.15183 Token Coherence; arXiv:2606.21666 Hallucination as Context Drift; arXiv:2405.03862 Persona Inconstancy; arXiv:2602.12285 From Biased Chatbots to Biased Agents; arXiv:2601.17152 Dynamic Role Assignment for Multi-Agent Debate; arXiv:2509.25271 RADAR. All 13 verified to resolve to those titles. Own transcripts: data/mixed-1, live-7, swarm-001301-leads and -hub-state-machine, swarm-093235-fsxs-leads, -done-caps-legacy, -tool-protocol, swarm-095400-1swa-evidence-and-privacy-verdict, swarm-232136-leads/-design/-evidence, recruit-live, plus src/hub.ts, src/server.ts, src/spawner.ts, src/swarm.ts and prompts/*.md at HEAD a8ae0b6.
rejected: a withdraw tool (reopens the re-propose path, loses on context cost); a concede tool and an object tool (absorbed by keeping a failed proposal open and by a challenge flag; lose on the chair's "new tool where a hint would do" criterion); refusing agree quotes byte-identical since v1 (punishes a stable core clause; false positives); clearing every vote on every amend (N re-votes per amend, mixed-1 had 17 amends); required role on join_room and one-verifier-per-room (re-appoint a verifier against done-means-independently-verified); spawning the chair as agent=human (self-declaration bypass and human register caps); Minority Sentinel's classifier to overturn majorities (illegible reason, larger than this list); an id-only proposal pointer (vote() would dump the full text on every quote mismatch).
revisit-when: after the seven HOW TO VERIFY checks pass, a run under the new rules either concludes over an unresolved_objections entry that later proves right, or shows the counted-voter full-text exception costing more than the gate saves; or a two-agent room under the 2+ gate stalls on a pro-forma challenge more than once per run.
RE-TARGET consensus-requires-scrutiny: the ruling's "in rooms of 3+ a proposal cannot pass until a non-proposer files a challenge" becomes 2+; evidence is that 9 of 25 concluded rooms held exactly two agents and ran with the gate mechanically off (hub.ts:1055), including 095400-evidence where the only challenge was voluntary and was dissolved by the challenger's own amend before consensus.

## Groups

### Tool surface and refusal messages: where the tools themselves misled or blocked us (concluded)

TOOL-SURFACE FINDINGS, ranked. All at HEAD a8ae0b6. Evidence in board evidence-A / evidence-B / stale-refusal-live.

1. A rejected proposal must stay open. hub.ts:1267 sets pr.status="rejected" on a failed tally; hub.ts:1026 then refuses amend; hub.ts:1268 says "Address the objections and propose a revision" — the exact move hub.ts:1068 forbids ("Do not re-propose: use amend"). mixed-1.jsonl seq 42 (codex-1, not the proposer) kills a 4-agree v7 document; seq 45 re-proposes the whole text as prop_df6ba59b, which is then amended 11 more times. swarm-232136-leads.jsonl seq 16/20/23/29/32 is the same loop five times: 69,081 chars of re-broadcast document and seq 17/21/30 voting disagree on one's own proposal to withdraw. Across data/*.jsonl, re-proposals equal "was rejected" notices 1:1 in 5 of 6 multi-proposal rooms; 92,674 chars total. CHANGE (enforce-in-hub): leave status "open" on a failed tally; post once "Proposal <id> did not pass (n agree / m disagree). Amend it in place: amend proposal_id=\"<id>\" find=\"<exact text>\" replace=\"<new text>\". Do not propose a new document." Re-arm that notice on the next amend so later votes do not repeat it. Effect: the edit history survives a single veto (quality); removes the largest measured context sink. No withdraw tool — it would reopen the re-propose path.

2. Every read-cursor advance must settleRead, not markRead. hub.ts:787 (pass) and the join cursor advance blindly markRead(latest), while only settleRead (hub.ts:509-515) preserves withheld/quiet delivery; swarms/swarm-001301/report.md "Hub concurrency F1" reproduces a human question dropped after a pass, and hub.ts:580-584 actively recommends pass. Separately, the stale-send guard (hub.ts:566-573) throws before any cursor moves and hub.read() never marks read, so read_messages cannot clear it: reproduced live in this room, two byte-identical refusals in a row, each repeating the unread batch. CHANGE (enforce-in-hub): call settleRead on the returned batch before throwing the stale-send refusal, and in pass/join; reword the refusal to "…read them (below). Then retry send_message with force=true, or call wait_for_messages." Effect: no dropped human message (quality, humans-answered-once axis); one refusal round-trip and one duplicated batch saved per collision.

3. Board text leaks into ordinary responses. hub.ts:308 serializes every entry's full text into summary, returned by join_room (server.ts:145-158) and room_status (server.ts:313-317); board_get with no key dumps every entry. data/swarm-001301-persistence-replay.jsonl seq 12/16 are 2,164 and 3,175-char entries. CHANGE (enforce-in-hub): summary and keyless board_get return {key, by, chars, updated_at}; text only via board_get(key) or include_board_text=true. Effect: pure context saving, no loss of access; keeps the board's one-line-notice contract (proposal-is-a-document).

4. Refusals must name the exact next call. hub.ts:568-572 and server.ts:178-182 say "Pass force=true" — `pass` is a real tool that takes no force (server.ts:285-293). Say "retry send_message with force=true". hub.ts:1038 echoes the entire proposal on a bad find ("…does not occur in the proposal … Current text:\n${pr.text}") — 19k chars for one typo in swarm-232136-leads; clip to the 200 chars around the closest match plus the version and length. The ack guidance (server.ts:384-395, hub.ts:1078-1079) says "write <key>.ack" without naming the tool; give the literal board_set(room, key:"<full-key>.ack", text:"ack"). All enforce-in-hub (they are hub-authored strings). Effect: removes retry loops and one worst-case 19k dump.

5. amend gains replace_all:boolean (default false) — replace the whole text, keeping the id, version chain and votes-reset semantics. Grounding: mixed-1.jsonl seq 42, codex-1's disagree reason asks to "adopt 'concise-replacement' on the board", i.e. a whole rewrite it had no way to apply; find="" only appends (hub.ts:1029). Enforce-in-hub (new param). Effect: makes item 1's "amend instead" reachable for a rewrite; avoids quoting a whole document as `find`.

6. Two description-only gaps (prompt-only, one line each). submit_opening (server.ts:204-206) never states the cap; hub.ts:750-751 enforces min(max_message_chars,400) — reproduced live: "Opening is 447 chars; openings are capped at 400." timeout_ms (server.ts:220-225) has no field description; add "milliseconds; omit for 25,000; 0 polls; max 55,000". Effect: one wasted round-trip per agent per room removed.

7. Persist tool refusals to the event log. hub.ts:671-691 persists hub events only; server.ts:79-87 discards MCP errors, so no transcript can evidence a wrong call, a retry or a bad timeout_ms — every claim above had to come from code reading or live reproduction. CHANGE (enforce-in-hub), narrowed after B's challenge: persist {type:"refusal", room, tool, reason_code} only — never the message body or the pid, since the bodies are exactly the 19k proposal dumps and unread batches item 4 removes, and a private failed attempt should not become permanent room data. Surface a per-room {tool, reason_code, count} aggregate in room_status; raw bodies only under an explicit bounded diagnostic mode. Effect: the next swarm ranks tool-surface fixes from data instead of re-deriving them.

8. Stop echoing the whole proposal on every call. wait_for_messages returns open_proposal.text (server.ts:246-248), room_status/join_room return it via proposalView inside summary (hub.ts:307), and amend returns the full new text as well as the diff (server.ts amend handler). Measured in this room: one 5.1k-char document, returned in full four times to me alone in eight calls — ~20k chars to re-read text I wrote. CHANGE (enforce-in-hub): return {id, version, by, tally, waiting_on, needs_challenge, challenges, chars} plus the last diff, and the text only on propose/amend-by-you, on the first wait after a version bump, or on request (room_status include_proposal_text=true). Effect: large context saving with no loss of scrutiny — the version number tells you when to re-read, and the diff already carries what changed (proposal-is-a-document).

HOW TO VERIFY: (1) in a 2-agent room, propose, have the other vote disagree, then amend — it must succeed and the notice must name amend; (2) send twice without force with unread present — the second call must succeed or the refusal must name wait_for_messages/force only; (3) pass after a withheld human message, then wait — the human message must still arrive; (4) join_room on a room with a 3k-char board entry must return chars, not text.

### Roles (chair, lead, verifier, recruit, human) and how sub-agents get briefed (concluded)

CONCRETE CHANGES TO FIX ROLE VISIBILITY AND RECRUITMENT CONTEXT (ranked by impact on convergence cost)

1. [HUB] Add role as first-class Participant field. src/hub.ts:20-37 Participant interface + join() at 354. Role enum: "worker" | "lead" | "verifier" | "recruit" | "chair". Default "worker". Role is an OPTIONAL join_room parameter defaulting to "worker": making it required would break prompts/participant.md, prompts/minimal.md and prompts/terse.md, none of which pass one. While in join_room, also return `settings_ignored: [...]` when options are supplied for a room that already exists — today src/hub.ts:209 discards them with no error, which is what makes item 8's race silent. Enforce at join: only one active "chair" per room (throw HubError if duplicate); multiple "verifier" roles are allowed (verifier is a label, not exclusive authority — verification authority is mechanical via different-connection + proposal-bound evidence at hub.ts:1241, per done-means-independently-verified). Cite: swarm-093235-fsxs-leads seq=25 (chair-claude had to announce role). Expected effect: eliminates 1-message role announcements, saves context in every message delivery.

2. [HUB] Surface role in every delivered message. src/hub.ts:388 (join announce), 739-757 (message formatting). Change from "X (agent) joined" to "X (agent, role: verifier) joined". Render role as a SEPARATE formatting tag, NOT folded into hub.shown() — shown() (src/hub.ts:264-266) is the anonymity primitive and is also what @mention resolution matches against (src/hub.ts:835,846-847), so appending a role to it would break mentions and leak into labels. Cite: data/swarm-093235-fsxs-tool-protocol.jsonl joins show tool-protocol-claude-2 = Participant A, lead -claude-1 = Participant B; labels do not track index. Expected effect: every room member always knows who is who; converges lead/lead-confusion in ~1 message instead of ~6. Context cost: ~10 chars per message delivery.

3. [HUB] Add role field to room_status (hub.summary). src/hub.ts:296-303 participants array. Add role to each participant dict. Expose per swarm.ts:33 (anonymous rooms use labels, reveal=false suppresses ids). Expected effect: leader can call room_status mid-discussion and see who is verifier, who are leads. Convergence: prevents re-introducing ghost-lead problems if a room is re-joined. Context cost: negligible.

4. [HUB] Add role parameter to request_agent. src/server.ts:468-488. New param: role="recruit" (default). Validate: only "recruit" is allowed (caps recruits from going straight to lead/verifier). spawner receives role and passes to recruit.md as {{ROLE}} (for documentation only; recruited agent cannot claim lead). Cite: src/spawner.ts:167-183. Expected effect: provides audit trail (list_agents shows requested_role) and brief is contextually accurate. Context cost: ~5 chars.

5. [HUB] Add role and connection-group to list_agents output. src/server.ts:501-508. Return array with: name, room, role, report_to, agent, model, requested_by, depth, running, connection_group (opaque per-room label indicating whether two agents share a connection, or "distinct" if verifiable that they do not — never expose session id or hash). Expected effect: chair can decide recruitment strategy and verifier eligibility under done-means-independently-verified (hub.ts:1241) is checkable before work starts (different connection required). Context cost: negligible (already lists name/agent/depth).

6. [HUB] Validate recruitment context and hide real names in anonymous rooms. src/spawner.ts:169-176. Add to request_agent optional field `area` (default unset) and required field `done_when` (what success looks like). spawner validates: if area is provided and not writable or already claimed, throw (consistent with claimArea). Render recruit.md with {{ROLE}}, {{LINEAGE}}, {{REPORT_TO}}, {{AREA}} (empty string if unset), {{ROOM_TOPIC}}, {{DONE_WHEN}}. When rendering into an anonymous room, render {{BY}} as hub.shown(room, requester) instead of requester.name. Cite: src/server.ts:493-495 leaks real name into recruit prompt; src/spawner.ts:170,176; data/recruit-live.jsonl seq=4-7 (16s with no area). Expected effect: recruits get full context (room, who asked, where to report, what done looks like); room anonymity is preserved. Context cost: ~40 chars added to recruit.md template.

7. [ENFORCE-IN-HUB] Chair role exemption from voting. src/hub.ts:1218-1220 unanimous branch; voters() currently counts every agent !== "human". The chair is NOT self-declared: add a room option `chair` (a participant name) settable only at room creation, exactly like quorum and anonymous — later joiners cannot change it because src/hub.ts:209 `if (existing) return existing`. role=="chair" is honoured only for the participant whose name matches that option. For that participant only, voters() excludes them and the humanVeto test at src/hub.ts:1210 becomes agent=="human" || role=="chair". This is strictly safer than the status quo: `agent` is unvalidated free text (src/hub.ts:355-380 validates only the display name), so today ANY connection can join as agent="human" and take the non-voter and veto branches with no room-level authorisation at all; and a chair that impersonates a human would also be routed through the withheld one-responder delivery at src/hub.ts:882-895 and the human register caps, per humans-answered-once-by-hub-enforcement. Cite: data/swarm-093235-fsxs-leads.jsonl seq=25 (chair-claude spends a message on "I will vote, so count me in the quorum") and seq=56 / docs/swarm-protocol-spec.md #56 ("leaving the room after this vote so my absence cannot block further amendments"). Expected effect: chair can stay active in leads room without deadlocking amendments. Convergence: saves 1 explicit leave+re-join sequence. Context cost: one line change.

8. [HUB + DEPLOYMENT] Fix verifier veto reliability: create leads room with options before launching agents. src/swarm.ts:232 backs verifier-first join only via comment and run.push order. Preconfigure the leads room through startup config or an explicit hub/launcher RPC call (hub lives in the child server at swarm.ts:95, so swarm.ts cannot call hub.createRoom directly). Calls to the hub's join_room after room creation must not fail if the room exists; if later options are ignored, return settings_ignored=[list of ignored options] so the orchestrator detects configuration races. Cite: hub.ts:209 `if (existing) return existing` silently discards a later joiner's options; verifier.md:1 claims a veto that is only mechanical via join-race luck. Expected effect: verifier's veto and require_verification gate become reliable. Context cost: preconfigure call (one-time at startup) and return clause on join. Update verifier.md:1 to describe the gate mechanism (connection-bound evidence) not a vote rule (hub-guaranteed).

Optional: [PROMPT] Add to lead-tail.md a note that if you are not the proposal's author, carry its text verbatim when reporting to the leads room. Cite: data/swarm-001301 et al., 8 of 14 concluded worker rooms had a non-lead author; measured, they re-summarized instead of citing. Expected effect: saves ~1 turn of the leads room repeating summaries. Context cost: 2 lines in lead-tail.md.

All changes respect identity-is-the-connection (role is display + voting authority only; verification gate remains connection-mechanical via different-connection at hub.ts:1241). All changes respect consensus-requires-scrutiny (role visibility does not change voting rules, only chair exemption which parallels the existing human exemption). No auction, no manager hierarchy.

### What wastes our context and wall-clock time (concluded)

RANKED WASTE SOURCES AND THE SIMPLEST FIX FOR EACH (measured from data/*.jsonl; no poll logs exist: swarms/*/*.log are 0 bytes and .out holds only the final CONCLUSION, so poll counts are bounds, not counts).

1. Conclusion message re-posts the full proposal text. src/hub.ts:1280 posts "CONSENSUS REACHED on id: ${pr.text}". 25/25 concluded rooms show 98-100% 8-gram overlap with the final proposal; 156,549 chars total; largest m_8343f638 (data/swarm-232136-leads.jsonl, 19,093 chars), m_0f79851e (data/swarm-093235-fsxs-leads.jsonl, 15,207). proposal+conclusion = 52.5% of all room text (749,253 chars); chat is 27%. FIX: post only "CONSENSUS REACHED on ${pr.id} v${pr.version} (${agree}/${n} agree)"; the text already lives in room.conclusion (hub.ts:1276) and room_status. ENFORCE-IN-HUB. Convergence risk: none, voting has already happened and room.conclusion still feeds swarm.ts and the report.

2. Every wait_for_messages return ships the full open_proposal.text and conclusion text, empty polls included. src/server.ts:246-256. data/swarm-093235-fsxs-leads.jsonl: a 15.8k-char proposal was open from seq 40 to 68, so a member polling once per incoming message received >=443k chars of proposal text against a 53k-char room log (this room reproduced it: the challenge's wait return carried the full 6.9k-char proposal again). FIX: add a per-participant field lastProposalVersionSeen (and lastConclusionSeen) set in wait(); include text only when the proposal id or version differs from what that participant was last returned, otherwise {id, version, tally, waiting_on, needs_challenge, text_omitted: "unchanged since vN; room_status for full text"}. Do NOT reduce to an id-only pointer: vote() checks the quote against live text (hub.ts:1146) and dumps the full text on mismatch, so a stale local copy would cost more round-trips exactly on the amend-heavy proposals. ENFORCE-IN-HUB. Convergence risk: low, amend already bumps version and resets votes, so nobody can vote on text they were not sent.

3. A refused send double-delivers. src/hub.ts:566-573 embeds every unread message in the refusal but never advances the cursor (unread() at hub.ts:441 reads lastSeenSeq without settling it), so the next wait_for_messages ships the same messages again, plus a 200-char slice in addressed_to_you. Reproduced in this room by B and by me (#10, #11: 1,271 chars delivered twice). FIX: call settleRead in the refusal path so the refusal counts as delivery. ENFORCE-IN-HUB. Convergence risk: none, the messages are in the error body the agent must read anyway.

4. Idle polling is doubled by the default and never measured. DEFAULT_WAIT_MS = 25s while MAX_WAIT_MS = 55s (src/server.ts:17-18) and no prompt passes timeout_ms; inter-message gaps >55s are 62-77% of sub-room wall time (fsxs sub-rooms 440-456s of 660-733s; 1swa sub-rooms 375-516s of 602-666s), a floor of 5-9 empty waits per member per sub-room. FIX: DEFAULT_WAIT_MS = 55_000, and record polls and empty_polls per participant in the hub (surfaced in room summary and the swarm report) so the next run counts instead of estimating. ENFORCE-IN-HUB. Convergence risk: none; wait() wakes on events, so latency to a human or a new message is unchanged.

5. Full-history re-delivery on demand with no "already delivered" marker. src/server.ts:301 read_messages defaults since_seq=0, limit=200: one call at the end of data/swarm-232136-leads.jsonl returns 160,953 chars the caller was already pushed; room_status returns every board entry in full (hub.ts:308; 50,563 chars in swarm-095400-1swa-leads). FIX: when participant_id is known, read_messages defaults since_seq to p.lastSeenSeq and every return carries delivered_up_to: N; room_status returns board keys with sizes and authors only, board_get fetches text. ENFORCE-IN-HUB. Convergence risk: none; since_seq=0 still works when history is wanted.

6. The brief is carried three times per worker. prompts/worker.md:6 puts {{DIRECTIVE}} in prose, prompts/worker.md:9 puts the same text in the join topic, and join_room echoes room.topic back (src/server.ts:147 via hub.summary). Directives are 1,109-2,340 chars (swarms/*/plan.json); for this run's 13 workers that is ~81k chars of which ~54k is repeat. FIX: worker.md:6 becomes "Your sub-question is the room topic in the join line below"; the topic stays in the room because humans, recruits and the dashboard read it there. PROMPT-ONLY. Convergence risk: none.

7. Undocumented 400-char opening cap costs round-trips. src/hub.ts:750 caps openings at min(maxMessageChars, 400) and :751 throws, while prompts/participant.md:7 and prompts/worker.md:9 say "under 60 words" and the submit_opening description says nothing; B hit the refusal twice in this room. FIX: submit_opening description and both prompts say "under 400 characters". PROMPT-ONLY. Convergence risk: none.

NOT WASTE, do not change: long chat is already solved by caps plus the board (only 4 chat messages >900 chars across the fsxs and 1swa runs, versus 32 messages / 73.7k chars in the pre-board 232136 run); blind openings are not duplicated (0-8% pairwise overlap in 23/25 rooms) and are the scrutiny mechanism; amend already posts diffs only (mean 407 vs 6,226 chars); board notices (mean 75 chars) already carry 275,196 chars off-chat; the one late leaver per sub-room is claude-1 (10/10 tail rooms) working in the leads room, not idling, so the post-conclusion tail is not a poll estimate.

VERIFY: after the changes, rerun the jq counts: every conclusion message under 200 chars; a wait_for_messages with no version change has no text field; a refused send followed by wait_for_messages returns zero seqs already in the refusal; polls and empty_polls appear per participant in the swarm report.

### Where convergence was shallow, premature, or stuck — and what new message kinds would fix it (concluded)

WHERE CONVERGENCE WAS SHALLOW, PREMATURE OR STUCK — ranked. Every item: grounding citation, precise change, effect on convergence quality AND context cost, enforce tag. Full measurements on the board under "evidence". No item weakens the challenge gate or the verification gate; none contradicts a settled axis.

1. AMEND MANUFACTURES AN UNQUOTED AGREE — enforce-in-hub.
GROUNDING: src/hub.ts:1043 `pr.votes = { [p.id]: { vote: "agree", ... reason: "amended to v${pr.version}" } }` — no quote, no reason, and it silently restores a vote that challenge() deleted at src/hub.ts:1122. data/swarm-095400-1swa-evidence-and-privacy-verdict.jsonl: seq14 claude-1 challenges (vote deleted), seq16 claude-1 amends (vote restored as agree, own challenge dissolved), seq17 claude-2 agrees, seq18 CONSENSUS — one quote-checked vote in the whole room. Same shape in data/swarm-093235-fsxs-done-caps-legacy.jsonl seq16 challenge → seq17 amend by the challenger → seq18 agree → seq19 conclusion. Measured explicit agree votes vs amends in concluded rooms: done-caps-legacy 4 amends/1 vote, 095400-evidence 2/1, 095400-hub-semantics-design 1/1, 093235-tool-protocol 7/2, live-7 13/3.
CHANGE (this is the single rule; items 1 and 2 are one edit to src/hub.ts:1042-1043, not two): an amend never creates or restores a vote, and it preserves an existing vote only while that voter's endorsed `quote` still appears verbatim in the new text — the amender included, and only if they had already cast a real vote. An amend by a participant with an unanswered challenge on the proposal does not clear that challenge; only their own explicit re-vote does.
EFFECT: quality — removes the one path by which a conclusion can be reached without the quote gate ever firing for a majority of the room, and makes consensus-requires-scrutiny true of the vote tally rather than only of the challenge count. Cost — strictly lower than today: the quote-survives clause is what saves mixed-1's 17 re-votes, so the rule pays for its own extra vote calls rather than leaning on item 3 to do it.

2. A LENGTH HEURISTIC DECIDES WHETHER A CHALLENGE STILL APPLIES — enforce-in-hub. (The vote half of this item is merged into item 1's single rule; what remains here is the challenge half and the regression evidence.)
GROUNDING: src/hub.ts:1039/1044 wipes every challenge on the proposal when the amended text changes by more than 25% of its length, and keeps every challenge when it changes by less — neither test looks at whether the challenged text survived. data/mixed-1.jsonl prop_df6ba59b reached v12 with 17 amends, 13 challenges and 17 votes for one conclusion. Regression is invisible to the hub: seq69 claude-2 — "v8's reason 3 reverts to the framing I challenged at v1" — an amend silently undid an already-answered objection and only a careful human-grade reader caught it.
CHANGE: scope the reset. An amend resets only the votes of participants whose endorsed `quote` no longer appears verbatim in the new text; everyone else's vote stands with a note "your quoted clause is unchanged". Same rule for challenges: `stale` clears only the challenges whose objection cites text that the amend removed, instead of all of them by a length heuristic.
EFFECT: quality — the vote tally stops being noise and starts meaning "this specific clause still has N endorsements"; it also makes item 3's regression check mechanical. Cost — the largest single saving available here: mixed-1's 17 votes collapse to roughly the number of amendments that actually touched a contested clause.

3. NO RECORD OF WHETHER AN OBJECTION WAS ANSWERED, AND NONE REACHES THE CONCLUSION — enforce-in-hub.
GROUNDING: `challenges: Challenge[]` (src/hub.ts:79, pushed at :1119) is append-only with no status field; conclude() (src/hub.ts:1273-1279) copies none of it. data/swarm-232136-leads.jsonl seq34, verifier challenges prop_a7e7aa68 with a substantive ask — "The assumption is that this answer has been verified. It has not... all six criteria begin 're-run a swarm and check'. Nobody in this room has run one" — and asks for one ordering sentence. It never enters the text. seq35 benji joins, seq36 AND seq37 benji votes AGREE (the same human vote recorded twice; no idempotence on the human vote path), seq39 verifier folds citing exactly that — "taking the ordering as implicit... and Benji has now joined and voted agree himself" — seq40 conclusion. `grep -ril "unresolved|unanswered objection" swarms/*/report.md` returns zero hits: the objection left no trace in any deliverable.
CHANGE: (a) give Challenge a status of open | answered | conceded | overruled; a challenge is answered only by an amend whose diff touches text the objection cites, or by the challenger's explicit re-vote carrying `resolves: <challenge_id>`. (b) conclude() emits `unresolved_objections: [...]` — every challenge not marked answered, verbatim with its author — into the conclusion message and into room_status, and the swarm report template carries the field through. (c) a repeat human vote on the same proposal is idempotent and does not re-post.
EFFECT: quality — the highest-leverage item on conclusion honesty: a room may still conclude over a live objection (nothing is blocked, the gates are untouched), but it can no longer do so silently, and the reader of swarms/*/report.md sees what was overruled. Cost — a few hundred chars at conclusion time; it removes the far larger cost of an objection being rediscovered in a later run.

4. UNTYPED DISSENT IS INVISIBLE TO evaluate() — enforce-in-hub (new message kind `object`).
GROUNDING: data/mixed-1.jsonl seq73, codex-1: "@claude-2 ... I cannot endorse 'Postgres undoable at any time' versus 'DynamoDB always requires migration'" — kind=chat, so evaluate() (src/hub.ts:1203) cannot see it. It was answered only because claude-2's v10 amend at seq72 crossed it by chance and they noticed at seq77. Earlier the same room forced the same content through the vote path instead: seq42 codex-1 votes DISAGREE purely to demand edits, rejecting a proposal with 4 agrees (seq43).
CHANGE: add `object(proposal_id, objection, blocking: bool)` — kind `object`. Non-blocking records dissent in the proposal record without touching the tally. Blocking holds the proposal exactly as an unanswered challenge does and is cleared only by the objector, by an explicit `concede(objection_id, reason)` or by a vote. It does NOT satisfy the challenge gate — a challenge is still required from a non-proposer in rooms of 3+.
EFFECT: quality — dissent stops depending on someone happening to read chat, and stops being laundered through DISAGREE votes that throw away four standing agreements. Cost — saves a full reject-and-re-propose cycle per instance; mixed-1 seq42/43 cost the room a re-proposal of an entire conclusion.

5. DISAGREE IS THE ONLY WAY TO SAY "YOU'RE RIGHT, PLEASE EDIT" — enforce-in-hub (new kind `concede`).
GROUNDING: 6 of 24 disagree votes across all data/*.jsonl are procedural rather than substantive, every one in swarm-232136: data/swarm-232136-design.jsonl seq11 "Withdrawing my own proposal so it can be superseded"; data/swarm-232136-evidence.jsonl seq13 "Disagreeing only to release the text for the two amendments you already accepted"; data/swarm-232136-leads.jsonl seq17, 21, 26, 30. Each produced a system "Proposal ... was rejected" and a full re-propose of a multi-thousand-character document to make an edit both parties already agreed on.
CHANGE: `concede(proposal_id, challenge_id | objection_id, reason)` — kind `concede`. Marks the cited objection conceded (feeding item 3), leaves the tally untouched, and grants the conceder a one-shot right to amend the cited span even if they are not the proposer.
EFFECT: quality — separates "I withdraw my objection" from "I reject this", which the tally currently conflates. Cost — removes the re-propose cycle; in 232136-leads that pattern drove five full proposal texts.

6. PRO-FORMA CHALLENGES AND TITLE-LINE AGREES SATISFY BOTH GATES — enforce-in-hub, and the strongest gate hardening here.
GROUNDING: challenge() validates only `objection.trim().length < 20` (src/hub.ts:1116). data/mixed-1.jsonl seq74, codex-3: "The riskiest assumption is the absence of a known DynamoDB-friendly workload... The proposal states that limitation and, for the given scope, this is acceptable" — concedes in the same sentence, then votes AGREE at seq75; same agent, same move at seq59 and seq63. On the vote side src/hub.ts:1146 checks only `norm(proposal.text).includes(norm(quote))`: in mixed-1, 11 of 16 agree votes quote the proposal's unchanged headline ("CONCLUSION: Choose Postgres, using a managed service." / "...managed PostgreSQL as the primary database."), the one clause untouched across all 12 versions, while every amend in the room edited reason 3. codex-3's five agree votes carry no `reason` at all.
CHANGE: (a) an agree vote's `quote` may not be a span that is byte-identical in v1 and in the current text when the proposal has ever been challenged — quote something that changed. (b) when a proposal has one or more challenges, an agree requires `reason` (20+ chars), mirroring the existing disagree rule at src/hub.ts:1150. (c) challenge tool description gains: "If you are about to concede in the same breath, do not challenge — vote, or file a non-blocking object."
EFFECT: quality — both gates currently certify the sentence nobody disputed; this points them at the contested clause, which is the whole purpose of consensus-requires-scrutiny. Cost — near zero; one extra field on votes that already carry prose in every non-codex case.

7. THE ROOM STALLS AND THE HUB KNOWS WHY BUT WILL NOT SAY — enforce-in-hub.
GROUNDING: src/hub.ts:1209 `if (room.expectedParticipants && this.voters(room).length < room.expectedParticipants) return;` — evaluate() returns silently. data/swarm-001301-hub-state-machine.jsonl room opts carry expectedParticipants=3 and only two agents ever joined. By seq14 both had agreed; the room then posted seq16, 17, 18, 20, 21, 22 — six identical "3 min of silence. Proposal prop_dea14e54 is open." The nudge (src/hub.ts:715-723) appends waiting_on and needs_challenge but has no branch for the quorum floor, the hold or the verification gate, so it named no blocker and no actor. Both agents left (seq19, seq23) and benji closed it at seq24: "Room closed by benji: stale run. No conclusion was recorded." The hub already knows how to name people — data/swarm-232136-evidence.jsonl seq4 "Still waiting for openings from Participant A", data/swarm-001301-leads.jsonl seq63/65/66/71 "still waiting for votes from verifier, orchestrator-launcher-claude-1" — only the blocked-propose path is mute.
CHANGE: every early return in evaluate() posts a one-line reason naming the blocker and the actor, once per state change: "prop_X has unanimous agreement but the room needs 3 participants and has 2 — nobody here can conclude it; request_agent or lower expected_participants." Make the nudge escalate rather than repeat: identical nudge text is posted at most twice, and the third fires a `stalled` state naming who must act. With no open proposal, name the participant who has spoken most and has not proposed, instead of "someone should propose a conclusion" (data/swarm-232136-leads.jsonl seq3, data/swarm-001301-leads.jsonl seq4/5).
EFFECT: quality — converts a dead room into either a conclusion or an explicit no-consensus; this room lost ~18 minutes and two agents' entire context and produced nothing. Cost — replaces six repeated nudges with two lines.

8. SUBMIT_OPENING'S 400-CHAR CAP IS UNDOCUMENTED AND UNRELATED TO THE ROOM SETTING — prompt-only.
GROUNDING: this session's tool result, join_room with max_message_chars=1400 accepted, then submit_opening refused: "Opening is 685 chars; openings are capped at 400. One or two sentences: your answer and the main reason." join_room's schema advertises max_message_chars as the cap on "chat/opening length".
CHANGE: state "openings are capped at 400 characters regardless of max_message_chars" in the submit_opening tool description and in join_room's max_message_chars description.
EFFECT: quality — none. Cost — one wasted round-trip per agent per room, paid by every agent in every swarm.

9. THE CHALLENGE GATE IS OFF IN MOST ROOMS THAT ACTUALLY CONCLUDE — enforce-in-hub.
GROUNDING: `challengeRequired()` (src/hub.ts:1055) returns `this.voters(room).length >= 3` under the "auto" default, so in any two-agent room the gate never fires. Measured over every concluded room in data/*.jsonl: 9 of 25 ran with two agents and the gate mechanically disabled — swarm-232136-design, swarm-232136-evidence, swarm-093235-fsxs-done-caps-legacy, swarm-093235-fsxs-tool-protocol, swarm-095400-1swa-evidence-and-privacy-verdict, swarm-095400-1swa-hub-semantics-design, swarm-001301-persistence-replay, swarm-224755-leads, live-2 — and a further 4 legacy rooms carried requireChallenge=null with zero challenges filed at all, including notes-storage-2 with four agents and swarm-224755-fix-slugify-truncation with three. This room is itself require_challenge:false. The disabled gate is what makes item 1 lethal rather than merely untidy: in swarm-095400-1swa-evidence-and-privacy-verdict the seq14 challenge was voluntary, so when the amend at seq16 dissolved it there was no rule left to stop seq18.
CHANGE: "auto" means two or more voters, not three. A two-agent room still needs one challenge from the non-proposer before a proposal can pass.
EFFECT: quality — restores consensus-requires-scrutiny to the 36% of concluded rooms where it is currently decorative; it is a strengthening of the challenge gate, not a weakening. Cost — one challenge call per two-agent room; every one of those nine rooms already filed one voluntarily, so the measured marginal cost is zero.

RANK ORDER (cost-to-fix over damage-prevented, as agreed in the room): 1, 7, 9, 6, 3, 2, 5, 4, 8. Items 1, 7 and 9 are one-line hub defects with the largest damage prevented per character changed; 6 and 3 are rules over existing structures; 2 is a real but larger refactor of amend; 5 and 4 add new kinds and new tools and so come last among the substantive items; 8 is a doc string.
RANKING RATIONALE: 1-3 are conclusion-integrity defects that let a room finish without the scrutiny the hub claims to enforce; 4-6 are the missing vocabulary and the gate hardening that keep real dissent visible and pro-forma dissent out; 7 is pure loss recovery; 8 is trivial but free.
HOW TO VERIFY: drive a scripted MCP session per item. (1) challenge then amend as the same participant and assert the proposal does not conclude. (2) amend a clause no voter quoted and assert their votes survive. (3) conclude a room with an open challenge and assert unresolved_objections is non-empty in the conclusion and in room_status. (4) file a blocking object and assert evaluate() will not conclude. (6) attempt an agree quoting only the unchanged v1 headline on a challenged proposal and assert refusal. (7) create a room with expected_participants=3, join 2, reach unanimity and assert a named blocker line is posted within one nudge interval.

### What we wish the hub told us: state, prompts and the guidance gap (concluded)

WHAT WE WISH THE HUB TOLD US — ranked. Every item is state the hub already computes and does not attach, or prompt wording measurably contradicted by a transcript. No scripted protocol is added to any prompt; all eight settled axes hold (items 1-6 and 8 are pure minimal-prompt-hub-carries-coordination: coordination moves OUT of prompts INTO hub payloads).

1. open_proposal.blocked_by + leaving_would_block — ENFORCE-IN-HUB.
Grounding: hub.ts:1209 `if (room.expectedParticipants && this.voters(room).length < room.expectedParticipants) return;` — evaluate() silently returns, and the nudge (hub.ts:720) says only "is open; still waiting for votes from ...". swarm-001301-leads #62 (00:38:51) had 5 of 7 agreed; #67-#70 four voters left within 12 min, each obeying the prompt line "If nothing happens for 10 minutes, leave"; prop_27e54a37 was then unpassable and benji closed the room with no conclusion at #72, 8h later. Same silence in swarm-001301-hub-state-machine #15-#18: both agreed, three nudges said only "is open", the room never learned it sat at 2 of 3 expected.
Change: wait_for_messages.open_proposal gains blocked_by: ["needs_challenge" | "needs_verify" | "quorum_floor: 5 voters, expects 7"] and leaving_would_block: true when your departure would drop voters below expected_participants; the same string goes in the hub.ts:720 nudge.
Convergence: converts a dead room into a concluded one — the single largest failure in the corpus. Context: +~25 tokens per poll.

2. One board index shape, everywhere — ENFORCE-IN-HUB.
Grounding: hub.ts:308 emits every board entry's FULL text in room_status: 35,712 chars over 8 keys in swarm-001301-leads, 45,480 over 9 in swarm-093235-fsxs-leads (~9-11k tokens per call). ui.ts:201 clampBlock()s exactly this data for the human; agents get it raw. Separately wait_for_messages returns board_keys (server.ts:242), names only, so a recruit cannot see who owns claim/<area> without a board_get per key.
Change: both hub.summary().board and wait_for_messages.board become [{key, by, updated_at, chars}] (plus parsed status for claim/*); board_get stays the only way to full text.
Convergence: recruits and verifiers see ownership and freshness in one call. Context: -9k to -11k tokens per room_status, and removes N board_get round-trips.

3. Version-stamped votes and challenges — ENFORCE-IN-HUB.
Grounding: vote()/challenge() (hub.ts:1109-1160) never record which version the caller read. mixed-1 #73 and #74 objected to reversibility wording that the v10 amend at #72 had already deleted; the proposer spent #77 explaining: "I'd already dropped it in v10, which landed while you were writing". 17 amends and 17 votes in that room. live-7 #24/#25 is the same bug in amend: two agents replaced the identical find-string 6s apart, and #38/#39 are both agents calling for an edit freeze after v9-v14 of churn.
Change: propose/amend/vote/challenge responses and the AMENDED chat notice all carry {version}; vote and challenge take an optional version and are refused when it is not current, returning the current text.
Convergence: no more votes and challenges aimed at deleted text. Context: down — kills the re-read-and-explain cycle.

4. Delete the room_status pointer in the amend notice — ENFORCE-IN-HUB.
Grounding: hub.ts:1048 appends "(votes reset; read the current text in room_status and re-vote)" to every amend, and room_status is the 35k-char call of item 2. wait_for_messages.open_proposal.text (server.ts:244) already carries the full current text. live-7 ran 13 amends in 60 seconds (#22 23:59:25 to #37 00:00:25), so the pointer fired 13 times.
Change: with item 3 in place the notice reads "(votes reset; current text v{n} is in your next wait_for_messages)". Context: removes one worst-case call per amend. Convergence: neutral.

5. openings on wait_for_messages, and fix the nudge fallthrough — ENFORCE-IN-HUB.
Grounding: hub.ts:292-294 computes the openings line for room_status and submit_opening returns {revealed, waiting_on} (hub.ts:757), but the wait_for_messages response object (server.ts:230-250) has no openings key at all — verified live in this room, where our #2 and #4 polls returned nothing about who had submitted. hub.ts:727 gates the openings nudge on `room.openings.size`, so a room with zero submitted falls through to the wrong branch: swarm-232136-evidence #3 at 23:25:44 told a pre-opening room "If the discussion has converged, someone should propose a conclusion", and that room sat 6 minutes (23:22:44 to 23:28:51) before reveal.
Change: add openings: {submitted, expected, waiting_on, revealed} to wait_for_messages; drop `&& room.openings.size` at hub.ts:727. Convergence: ends blind pre-opening polling and one actively wrong instruction. Context: +~15 tokens, zero for the nudge fix.

6. humans_present — ENFORCE-IN-HUB, plus a prompt cut.
Grounding: the hub knows every participant with agent === "human" and relabels them in anonymous rooms, yet minimal.md:5 hedges "A human may be watching and may join in." and terse.md:5 "A human may join in."; unanswered_human (server.ts:238) goes null the moment a human is answered, so presence is unknowable thereafter.
Change: attach humans_present: [names] to join_room and wait_for_messages; delete both sentences. Convergence: agents stop guessing whether to keep a register. Context: -2 prompt sentences, +~8 tokens.

7. PROMPT-ONLY edits, two sentences quoted verbatim.
(a) worker.md:19 / participant.md:15 "If nothing happens for 10 minutes, leave." is actively misleading — it caused #67-#70 in item 1. Replace with: "Leave when the hub says the room concluded or closed, or when it tells you leaving would not block an open proposal; if it would block and nothing has happened for 20 minutes, say so in the room and leave anyway." One sentence pointing at a hub field with a bound, not a protocol; the bound is what stops a never-voting participant (swarm-001301-leads #66/#71, verifier never voted) from hanging everyone until a human closes the room. The 20 is the one number here that is chosen, not measured: swarm-001301-leads brackets it at 15 min from #62 to the last leave and 8h to the human close. Treat it as a knob.
(b) verifier.md:14 "if all groups have reported and no lead proposes within a few rounds" — room.round only advances via advanceTurn, reached only from hub.ts:587 (send) and hub.ts:790 (pass), both gated on round_robin; the leads room is free mode, so round is permanently 1 and "a few rounds" is undefined. Replace "within a few rounds" with "after the hub's second silence nudge" (hub.ts:730).

8. Tell the voter when a disagree will reject, not reopen — ENFORCE-IN-HUB.
Grounding: this room, prop_c90e3c0b: the wait_for_messages hint (server.ts) said "disagree with the specific change you need (or just amend it)", so Participant B voted disagree with two concrete edits and hub.ts:1219 `if (disagree > 0) rejected = true;` (the unanimous branch of evaluate) rejected the proposal outright, forcing a re-propose of ~9k chars instead of a two-line amend; the later hub.ts:1233-1235 late-joiner clause never ran, since accepted was already false. amend() then refused with "Proposal prop_c90e3c0b is rejected; only open proposals can be amended." The hub knew the vote was decisive; the voter did not.
Change: wait_for_messages.open_proposal gains your_vote_decides: "disagree rejects it now; amend instead if you want a change", computed from quorum and waiting_on; the vote tool's hint says the same when the caller's vote would close the tally. Convergence: keeps one proposal amended in place (proposal-is-a-document). Context: -1 re-propose per near-unanimous room.

DROPPED, not weakened: "Never set timeout_ms above 55000" (worker.md:19, participant.md:15, minimal.md:5, terse.md:9, recruit.md:11, verifier.md:17) is NOT dead weight. server.ts:232 clamps with Math.min(timeout_ms ?? DEFAULT_WAIT_MS, MAX_WAIT_MS), but server.ts:223's zod `.max(MAX_WAIT_MS)` rejects a larger value as a hard InputValidationError before the clamp is reached, and the six copies are not duplication: swarm.ts:56/237/285 and spawner.ts:149 render worker.md, recruit.md and verifier.md as different agents' entire prompts, so each copy is load-bearing for its own agent. Removing the zod max is a refusal change and belongs to the tool-surface room; if they remove it, the sentence goes with it.

NOT PROPOSED, deliberately: the unanswered "Hello team!" at live-4 #5 and the double reply to benji at live-7 #41/#42 both predate commits a9e0981 and 967595d (responder nomination, 2026-09-17) and are already fixed; citing them would overstate the gap.

```

Every other item strengthens a settled axis rather than contradicting one.

Measured over all 33 rooms in data/*.jsonl: 98-100% 8-gram overlap with the final proposal in 25 of 25 concluded rooms, 156,549 chars — 20.9% of all room text; largest m_8343f638 (swarm-232136-leads, 19,093 chars, verifier-confirmed). CHANGE: post `CONSENSUS REACHED on <id> v<version> (<agree>/<n> agree)`; the text already lives in room.conclusion (hub.ts:1276), room_status and the dashboard. QUALITY: unchanged — voting is over when this fires. CONTEXT: -20.9% of room text, broadcast to every member. ENFORCE-IN-HUB.
    
    2. A FAILED TALLY MUST LEAVE THE PROPOSAL OPEN, AND THE NOTICE MUST NAME AMEND. hub.ts:1267 sets status="rejected"; hub.ts:1026 then refuses amend; hub.ts:1268 says "Address the objections and propose a revision" — the exact move hub.ts:1068 forbids ("Do not re-propose: use amend"). data/mixed-1.jsonl seq 42: codex-1, not the proposer, kills prop_2d212b1e at v7 after 4 agrees and six amendments; seq 45 restarts the whole document as prop_df6ba59b, then 11 more amends. data/swarm-232136-leads.jsonl seq 16/20/23/29/32: five documents, 13,822→19,057 chars, 69,081 chars re-broadcast, and seq 17/21/30 are the author voting disagree on its own proposal ("Withdrawing my own prop_9aa33318") because there is no other exit. Across data/*.jsonl, re-proposals equal "was rejected" notices 1:1 in 5 of 6 multi-proposal rooms; 92,674 chars (verifier's independent count 92.9k). CHANGE: keep status "open" — but NOT on the claim that the tally alone blocks acceptance, which context-waste showed is false once the document stays open: leave() re-evaluates every open proposal (hub.ts:407), voters() counts only active participants (hub.ts:422-423), so the lone dissenter walks out, their disagree vanishes at hub.ts:1216, everyoneVoted goes true over the remaining agrees and hub.ts:1259 concludes the document the room rejected — something today's status="rejected" makes impossible at hub.ts:1204, and our own prompts tell that dissenter to leave after ten quiet minutes. So: on a failed tally record pr.blockedBy = [the dissenting participant ids] and have evaluate() refuse to accept while any entry stands, clearing an entry only when that agent re-votes or the text is amended in a way that touches what they objected to (the same survives-verbatim test as item 7(b)). This is item 7(d) in another guise — an objection must outlive the agent who filed it. Then post once "Proposal <id> did not pass (n agree / m disagree). Amend it in place: amend proposal_id=\"<id>\" find=\"<exact text>\" replace=\"<new text>\". Do not propose a new document."; re-arm on the next amend; and warn in the same words when a disagree first lands, before the tally closes (hub-telemetry item 8). QUALITY: the edit history survives a single veto instead of restarting. CONTEXT: the largest re-broadcast sink in the corpus. ENFORCE-IN-HUB. We deliberately did NOT add a withdraw tool — it restores the re-propose path.
    
    3. SHIP THE PROPOSAL TEXT ONLY WHEN IT CHANGED. server.ts:249 returns open_proposal.text and :264 the conclusion unconditionally, empty returns included; hub.ts:307 does the same inside every join_room/room_status summary, and amend returns the whole new text beside its diff. In data/swarm-093235-fsxs-leads.jsonl prop_55e859d3 grew to 15,171 chars and stayed open across 31 further messages (≥443k chars of proposal against a 53k-char log); measured live in this swarm, one 5.1k document came back in full four times in eight calls. CHANGE: per-participant lastProposalVersionSeen/lastConclusionSeen; send {id, version, tally, waiting_on, needs_challenge, challenges, chars, text_omitted:"unchanged since vN"} when unchanged. NOT an id-only pointer: vote() checks the quote against live text (hub.ts:1146) and dumps the whole document on mismatch (:1147). QUALITY: unchanged, but only if item 7 ships with it. The safety argument is "amend bumps the version and resets votes, so nobody votes on text they were not sent" — and today hub.ts:1043 exempts the amender, installing their own unquoted agree. Gate the text without fixing that and you get exactly one agent voting on text nobody else was sent. Ship 3 and 7 together — and note that 7(a)'s quote-survives clause reopens the same hole from the other side, because a preserved agree can now outlive an amend its author never saw while this item stops pushing them the new text. So the gate carries one exception: a participant whose vote is currently counted toward an open proposal is always sent the full text when the version moves, whatever their last-seen version. That is one document per voter per amend rather than per poll, and it is the copy worth paying for; 7(a) should say in the same breath that a preserved vote is preserved against a quote, not against a document, and show it in the tally as "agreed at vN" beside the current version. CONTEXT: removes the dominant per-poll cost. ENFORCE-IN-HUB.
    
    4. SETTLE THE READ CURSOR WHEREVER THE HUB CLAIMS DELIVERY. The stale-send guard (hub.ts:566-573) embeds every unread message in the refusal but throws before the cursor moves, and hub.read() never marks read, so read_messages cannot clear it — reproduced independently by three of us today (two byte-identical refusals in swarm-102607-irkd-tool-surface; the same again in context-waste, plus a third copy of one message in addressed_to_you; and the chair read_messages #3-#36 in this room, after which its next wait_for_messages re-delivered all 8k chars of them — so `read` never settles the cursor either, not only the refusal path). Separately hub.ts:787 (pass) and the join cursor advance call markRead(latest) instead of settleRead (hub.ts:509-515), which is how swarms/swarm-001301/report.md "Hub concurrency F1" drops a human question after a pass — while hub.ts:580-584 actively recommends pass. CHANGE: settleRead on the delivered batch in the refusal path, in pass and on join; reword the refusal to "…read them (below). Then retry send_message with force=true, or call wait_for_messages." QUALITY: no dropped human message (humans-answered-once-by-hub-enforcement); the guard stays cheap to trigger, which is what we want. CONTEXT: halves every collision. ENFORCE-IN-HUB.
    
    5. BOARD ENTRIES TRAVEL AS A MANIFEST, NOT AS TEXT. hub.ts:308 serializes every entry's full text into summary, returned by join_room (server.ts:145-158) and room_status (server.ts:313-317); keyless board_get dumps everything; read_messages defaults since_seq=0 (server.ts:297, verifier-corrected), which returned 160,953 already-delivered chars in one call at the end of swarm-232136-leads. Board dumps measured at 35,712 and 45,480 and 50,563 chars — and these sit outside the 749,253-char room-text census, so they are additive. ui.ts:201 already clamps exactly this data for the human reader. CHANGE: summary and keyless board_get return [{key, by, chars, updated_at}]; text via board_get(key) or include_board_text=true; read_messages defaults since_seq to p.lastSeenSeq and every return carries delivered_up_to. Drop hub.ts:1048's "read the current text in room_status" (live-7 fired it 13 times in 60s) now that item 3 pushes changed text. QUALITY: unchanged, nothing becomes unreachable. CONTEXT: large, on every call. ENFORCE-IN-HUB.
    
    6. TELL US WHO IS BLOCKING AND WHETHER LEAVING BLOCKS. evaluate() returns silently at hub.ts:1209 and the nudge at hub.ts:720 says only that a proposal "is open". data/swarm-001301-leads.jsonl #62: 5 of 7 agreed; #67-#70 four voters leave, obeying "If nothing happens for 10 minutes, leave"; prop_27e54a37 becomes unpassable and benji closes it by hand at #72, eight hours later. Same mechanism in data/swarm-001301-hub-state-machine.jsonl seq 16-24: unanimous and dead through six identical nudges. CHANGE: open_proposal gains blocked_by:[names] and leaving_would_block:boolean; leave_room refuses once, naming which of the two it is — "Your agree is the last one prop_X needs" for a missing agree, or "Your disagree is the only thing holding prop_X open; leaving would let it pass" for the case context-waste found, where leave() re-evaluates (hub.ts:407), voters() drops the departed (hub.ts:422-423) and the dissent vanishes from the tally at hub.ts:1216; every evaluate() no-op that leaves a proposal stuck posts why. Both halves are needed together with item 2's pr.blockedBy: keeping a failed proposal open without them turns walking out into a way to pass it, which today's status="rejected" makes impossible at hub.ts:1204. And the prompt sentence that produced #67-#70 is replaced in item 10, not left standing — this item supplies the field that sentence must point at. QUALITY: rooms stop dying at 5/7 without creating a room that concludes because its dissenter left. CONTEXT: a few dozen chars against hours of wall-clock. ENFORCE-IN-HUB.
    
    7. AMEND MUST NOT VOTE FOR YOU, AND VOTES MUST BE VERSION-STAMPED. hub.ts:1043 makes the amender an unquoted agree and hub.ts:1122 revives the vote challenge() had just cleared: in data/swarm-095400-1swa-evidence-and-privacy-verdict.jsonl the challenge at seq 14, the challenger's own amend at seq 16 and consensus at seq 18 ride on a single quote-checked vote (verifier-confirmed; same shape in swarm-093235-fsxs-done-caps-legacy seq 16-19). Unversioned objections cost a second round too: mixed-1 #73/#74 object to text deleted at #72 ("I'd already dropped it in v10, which landed while you were writing", #77). CHANGE, four edits on the same evaluate()/amend path, answering both challenges. (a) hub.ts:1042-1043: an amend never creates or restores a vote, and preserves an existing vote only while that voter's endorsed `quote` still appears verbatim in the new text — the amender included, and only if they had actually voted. The quote-survives half is what keeps this cheap (the chair is right that clearing everyone costs N re-votes per amend, and mixed-1 ran 17 amends for one conclusion); an amend by someone with an unanswered challenge does not clear it, only their explicit re-vote does. A preserved vote is preserved against a QUOTE, not against a document — context-waste is right at #61 that otherwise 7(a) plus item 3's version gate lets an agree survive an amend the voter was never sent — so the tally must render it as "agreed at vN" beside the current version, and item 3 must always push the full text to any participant whose vote is currently counted toward the proposal when the version moves, regardless of the gate. That is one document per counted voter per amend, not per poll, and it is the copy worth paying for. (b) hub.ts:1039/1044: replace the "changed more than 25% of the text length" heuristic with the same survives-verbatim test — clear a challenge only when the text its objection cites is gone, and reopen a cleared challenge if a later amend reintroduces that text; data/mixed-1.jsonl seq 69, "v8's reason 3 reverts to the framing I challenged at v1", is an answered objection silently undone, caught today only by one agent's memory. (c) RE-TARGET consensus-requires-scrutiny: the recorded ruling reads "in rooms of 3+ a proposal cannot pass until a non-proposer files a challenge"; we move that threshold to 2+. Evidence: the gate was mechanically off in 9 of the 25 concluded rooms because they held exactly two agents. The direction is toward more scrutiny, not less, but it edits a settled parameter, so it carries the label rather than hiding behind "strengthening". hub.ts:1055: "auto" means two or more voters, not three. In 9 of the 25 concluded rooms in data/*.jsonl the challenge gate never fired because only two agents were present — swarm-232136-design, -evidence, 093235-done-caps-legacy, 093235-tool-protocol, 095400-evidence-and-privacy-verdict, 095400-hub-semantics-design, 001301-persistence-replay, 224755-leads, live-2 — plus 4 legacy rooms with requireChallenge=null and zero challenges filed at all, including notes-storage-2 with four agents. This is what makes this item's own grounding lethal: in 095400-evidence the seq 14 challenge was voluntary, so when the seq 16 amend dissolved it, no rule was left to stop seq 18. (d) Challenge gains a status (open|answered|conceded|overruled), answered only by an amend whose diff touches the text the objection cites or by the challenger's re-vote naming it; conclude() (hub.ts:1273-1279) emits unresolved_objections — every still-open challenge, verbatim with its author — into the conclusion and room_status, and the report template carries it. data/swarm-232136-leads.jsonl seq 34: the verifier's "The assumption is that this answer has been verified. It has not... Nobody in this room has run one" asked for one ordering sentence, never got it, and the room concluded at seq 40 after the verifier folded at seq 39 citing benji's agree at seq 36/37 (the same human vote recorded twice — make repeat human votes idempotent). `grep -ril "unresolved|unanswered objection" swarms/*/report.md` returns zero hits. Also add challenge(blocking:false) as an optional flag — NOT a new tool — so dissent like data/mixed-1.jsonl seq 73 ("I cannot endorse...", kind=chat and therefore invisible to evaluate()) is recorded and carried into (d) without holding the tally; it does not satisfy the challenge gate. Finally, while a challenge is open an agree requires `reason` (20+ chars), mirroring the disagree rule at hub.ts:1150: in data/mixed-1.jsonl 9 of 16 agree votes quote only the headline that never changed across 12 versions, and codex-3's 4 of those carry no reason at all (verifier-corrected counts). We dropped the stronger form of that rule — refusing any quote byte-identical in v1 and the current text — because the chair is right that it punishes quoting a stable core clause, and reason-required alone catches every reason-less vote. QUALITY: this is the only item on the list that changes what a conclusion means rather than what it costs — it closes the path to consensus with no quote-checked vote, turns the gate on in the third of rooms where it is decorative, and stops a live objection vanishing from the record. CONTEXT: net negative — (a)'s quote-survives clause removes the re-vote storm, (c) is measured at zero marginal cost since all nine of those rooms already challenged voluntarily, and (d) costs a few hundred chars at conclusion. ENFORCE-IN-HUB. On axes: (d) strengthens consensus-requires-scrutiny and nothing here weakens the challenge or verification gate, but (c) edits a settled parameter and is labelled RE-TARGET consensus-requires-scrutiny above — the earlier "no axis is touched" line was left standing after that label went in and is withdrawn.
    
    8. REFUSALS MUST NAME A CALL THAT EXISTS. hub.ts:568-572 and server.ts:178-182 say "Pass force=true to send anyway" — `pass` is a real tool that takes no force (server.ts:285-293); say "retry send_message with force=true". hub.ts:1033 echoes the ENTIRE proposal on a bad find ("…does not occur in the proposal. Copy the exact text to replace. Current text:\n${pr.text}") — 19k chars for one typo against the swarm-232136-leads document; clip to ~200 chars around the closest match plus version and length. The ack guidance (server.ts:384-395, hub.ts:1078-1079) says "write <key>.ack" without naming a tool; give board_set(room, key:"<full-key>.ack", text:"ack"). Add amend replace_all:boolean (default false) so a whole rewrite is reachable without quoting the document as `find` — mixed-1 seq 42 asked to "adopt 'concise-replacement' on the board" and had no way to apply it (find="" only appends, hub.ts:1029). QUALITY: removes retry loops. CONTEXT: one worst-case 19k dump per typo. ENFORCE-IN-HUB.
    
    9. THE HUB SHOULD CARRY THE ROLE IT ALREADY DEPENDS ON. `grep -rn "chair" src/` returns one hit, the comment at hub.ts:421; leadership is an orchestrator loop index (at HEAD, swarm.ts:267 `const isLead = i === 1;`; the working tree shows :283 `!FLAT && i === 1` because the chair added a --flat launcher mode mid-run — verifier-corrected twice, and the second correction is the one to trust) that never reaches the hub, and the anonymous labels do not track it — live this run, join_room returned "you_are": "Participant C" to a designated lead, so the other two could not know who carries the conclusion (that 7 of 13 accepted proposals in the pre-run corpus were written by a non-lead — 10 of 18 counting this run's five rooms, including this one, whose prop_a1c84ae8 its lead did not write — is context for why that matters, not itself the cost; the chair is right about that. My first figure of 8 of 14 was wrong: it read the last proposal message rather than matching CONSENSUS REACHED back to its proposal id, which mis-scored swarm-224755-fix-slugify-truncation and counted swarm-001301-hub-state-machine, a room that never concluded. Corrected on the verifier's challenge.). Because hub.ts:423 counts every non-human participant as a voter, a chair must leave to avoid blocking: data/swarm-093235-fsxs-leads.jsonl seq 25 spends a whole message declaring the role and seq 56 says "I am leaving the room after this vote so my absence cannot block further amendments." CHANGE: optional role on join_room (default "worker"; chair|lead|verifier|recruit), shown as a tag in fmt and list_agents, and, for a chair only, excluded from voters() (hub.ts:423) while its disagree still counts, by extending the humanVeto test at hub.ts:1210 to `agent=="human" || role=="chair"`. "unless it votes" would re-create the deadlock: the chair in 093235-leads DID vote, twice, and still had to leave. The chair is NOT self-declared — it is named by a `chair` room option settable only at room creation, like quorum and anonymous (hub.ts:209 `if (existing) return existing`), and role="chair" is honoured only for the participant whose name matches; that binding is what makes the "strictly safer" note below true. Also fix server.ts:493, which passes the recruiter's REAL name to the spawner while server.ts:495 correctly uses hub.shown() — a recruit's first @-reply writes a real name into an anonymous room. isLead is swarm.ts:267 at HEAD (`const isLead = i === 1;`); the verifier's 283 and v18's 310 read the dirty working tree, which the chair confirms is its own mid-run --flat edit, so item 10's SETTLED line is swarm.ts:285 at HEAD. RESEARCH, done on benji's instruction, four sources, none among the 43 already read, each abstract fetched; full notes on the board as research/roles. It cuts partly against me and I report that rather than bury it. arXiv:2405.03862 (Persona Inconstancy) finds agents fail to hold an assigned persona under perceived peer pressure, and debate-framed instructions raise the inconstancy rate — that is positive support for the ENFORCE-IN-HUB tag specifically, because a prompted role is precisely what gets dropped, and chair-claude re-asserting its role at seq 25 then abandoning it at seq 56 is that failure in our own log. arXiv:2602.12285 finds role assignment measurably shifts behaviour on multi-step agentic tasks, so the role field must stay a display tag plus one quorum rule and must never be rendered into an agent's prompt as a character to play. AGAINST me: arXiv:2601.17152's gains (up to 74.8% over uniform, 29.7% over random) and RADAR's ablation (arXiv:2509.25271, role-free debate beats independent voting yet trails specialised roles) both come from role SPECIALISATION — matching competences — which this item does not buy; it makes an existing allocation visible, it does not choose one, and choosing one is an appointment mechanism we ruled out. So item 9 WITHDRAWS any claim to a convergence gain on the literature's authority: no source measures a display-only role field or a chair exempted from quorum. QUALITY, on our transcripts alone: the chair stops having to leave to unblock amendments, and the room can see who carries its conclusion. CONTEXT: one message per chair per room, plus the re-read when a non-lead's text must be re-summarised. ENFORCE-IN-HUB. Note hub.ts:355-380 never validates `agent`, so any connection can already claim agent="human" and take the veto branch (hub.ts:1210): a named role is strictly safer than the status quo. We dropped "require role", "one verifier per room" and "spawn the chair as agent=human" rather than defend them.
    
    10. PROMPT-ONLY BUNDLE (one line each). submit_opening (server.ts:204-206) never states that hub.ts:750-751 caps openings at min(max_message_chars, 400) — two of us hit "Opening is 447 chars; openings are capped at 400." live today; say "under 400 characters" in the description and in participant.md/worker.md. timeout_ms (server.ts:220-225) has no field description: "milliseconds; omit for 25,000; 0 polls; max 55,000" — and raise DEFAULT_WAIT_MS to 55,000 (server.ts:17), since gaps over 55s are 62-77% of sub-room wall time. worker.md:6 stops repeating the directive that worker.md:9 already passes as the room topic and join_room echoes back (~54k of ~81k brief chars this run). spawner.ts:167-183 gives a recruit no TASK, no DONE_WHEN, no topic and no settled axes where the worker path gives all four (swarm.ts:285 at HEAD, :310 in the dirty working tree); pass them. And the sentence item 6 blames for killing prop_27e54a37 must actually be removed: worker.md:19 and participant.md:15 still read "If nothing happens for 10 minutes, leave." — replace with "Leave when the hub says the room concluded or closed, or when it tells you leaving would not block an open proposal; if it would block and nothing has happened for 20 minutes, say so in the room and leave anyway." Without this the list diagnoses a prompt cause and ships only a hub field, and a leave_room refused "once" is exactly what an agent under a 10-minute instruction retries past. The 20 is a knob, not a measurement: swarm-001301-leads brackets it at 15 min (#62 to the last leave) and 8h (benji's close). PROMPT-ONLY except DEFAULT_WAIT_MS.
    
    CITATION CORRECTIONS at HEAD, from the verifier and re-checked by me with sed: the bad-find dump in item 8 is hub.ts:1033, not 1038; read_messages is server.ts:297-300 (its since_seq default of 0 is on the inputSchema line), not 301; the worker's `SETTLED + worker.md` in item 10 is swarm.ts:285 at HEAD (:310 in the dirty working tree), not 284; isLead is swarm.ts:267 at HEAD (:283 in the working tree, after the chair's mid-run --flat edit). Item 9's non-lead-author count stands as the verifier's 7 of 13, not my 8 of 14, and it is context either way.
    
    EVIDENCE STATUS, answering benji at m_92c719a1. Every DEFECT above is reproduced rather than reasoned: each was read at HEAD and, where a number appears, counted over all 33 rooms in data/*.jsonl. The FIXES are a different class of claim — they are design choices this corpus cannot settle, and we did not consult the literature on them. The three that most need it, none covered by the 43 sources in our settled block: whether preserving a vote against a surviving quote (7(a)) is sound or merely cheap; whether carrying unresolved objections into a conclusion (7(d)) changes what anyone downstream does, or only what the record says; and whether exempting a chair from quorum (item 9) helps more than a chair who simply abstains. Treat those three as proposals to test, not as findings. On benji's instruction I then went and looked, and the literature bears on two of them — four sources, none in the 43 on the settled list, each abstract fetched rather than taken from a search snippet.
    SUPPORTS 7(d). arXiv:2606.29270 "Minority Sentinel: When to Overturn Majority Voting in Multi-Agent LLM Debates": "roughly one in four divergent cases has the minority holding the correct answer, yielding a 10-percentage-point theoretical recovery margin", attributed to correlated errors across modern LLMs violating the independence assumption the Condorcet Jury Theorem needs. That is the strongest argument in this document for unresolved_objections: if a quarter of outvoted minorities are right, a conclusion that silently discards the objection discards the answer a quarter of the time. It also raises the stakes on 7(c) — a room of near-identical Claude instances is precisely the correlated-error case, which is the mechanism, not just the count, behind the gate being off in 9 of 25 concluded rooms.
    SUPPORTS THE seq 39 READING. arXiv:2604.02668 "Too Polite to Disagree: Understanding Sycophancy Propagation in Multi-Agent Systems" finds agreement bias cascades between agents and that countering it with peer sycophancy priors "improves final discussion accuracy by an absolute 10.5%". data/swarm-232136-leads.jsonl seq 39 is a textbook instance: the verifier folds a live objection in the sentence immediately after noting "Benji has now joined and voted agree himself". Related: arXiv:2608.02758 on pluralistic ignorance in LLM populations.
    CUTS AGAINST ITEM 9, reported rather than buried. arXiv:2601.10102 "When Identity Overrides Incentives / Role Identity Bias" finds assigned role personas suppress payoff-aligned behaviour in four-agent games, shifting equilibrium attainment by up to 90 percentage points even with complete information, and defines role identity bias as adhering to persona-consistent action against explicit goals. Item 9 as written is narrow enough to survive this — it is a hub field for quorum accounting and display, not a persona injected into a prompt — but the paper is a direct warning against any version of item 9 that puts a role into the agent's instructions, and it should be read before that line is drawn.
    NOT ADDRESSED BY ANY OF THESE: whether a vote preserved against a surviving quote (7(a)) is sound. That one remains untested. One item does now have outside support, found after benji's note and not among the 43: arXiv:2606.05037, "Self-Reflective APIs: Structure Beats Verbosity for AI Agent Recovery" (Canedo and Chethan), argues that an error response carrying the callable name and the parameters needed to recover outperforms verbose explanation of what went wrong — which is exactly item 8's shape ("retry send_message with force=true" rather than "Pass force=true", board_set(key:"<key>.ack") rather than "write <key>.ack") and exactly the case against hub.ts:1033 answering a typo with 19k chars of proposal. arXiv:2509.25370 and arXiv:2508.11027 are the adjacent failure-recovery evidence; neither is in our settled list either. This does not make items 3, 7(a), 7(d) or 9 research-backed — they are still ours to test.
    
    HONEST LIMITS, recorded so nobody re-derives them. No refusal is ever persisted (hub.ts:671-691 logs hub events; server.ts:79-87 discards MCP errors) and swarms/*/*.log are 0 bytes, so every poll figure above is a bound and no JSONL seq can evidence a refusal — the refusal findings here come from code reading and live reproduction. Hence the eleventh change we would make if the list were longer: persist {type:"refusal", room, tool, reason_code} — no body, no pid — and expose a {tool, reason_code, count} aggregate. Withdrawn on challenge: context-waste's 139 agent-minutes of idling (those leads were working in this room, as lead-tail.md instructs) and roles' claim that recruits burn turns asking (data/recruit-live.jsonl: joined seq 4, reported by seq 6, left seq 7, 16 seconds).
    
    TWO MORE SOURCES, on the delivery items rather than the voting ones, so this paragraph does not repeat the block above — it adds only what EVIDENCE STATUS lacks, and both abstracts were fetched, not taken from a snippet; full notes on the board as research/context-waste-sources. arXiv:2603.15183 "Token Coherence" names items 1/3/5's pathology in our own words — FULL-STATE REBROADCAST, "the orchestrator injects the complete updated artifact into every agent's prompt", cost O(n × S × |D|) in agents × steps × artifact size — and fixes it exactly as item 3 does, with lazy invalidation and version tokens instead of resend, O(n × S × |D|) → O((n + W) × |D|); we cite its shape, not its 81-95% savings, which are simulated, because our own 156,549 measured chars are the stronger evidence. arXiv:2606.21666 answers the obvious objection empirically: selective synchronization beat full broadcast on hallucination while making 58% fewer API calls, gated on divergence rather than on a timer — which is also why item 3's exception is right, since a counted voter has by definition diverged when the version moves. arXiv:2606.29270 "Minority Sentinel" is the grounding for item 2's pr.blockedBy and item 7(d): across three-agent debates on six benchmarks roughly ONE IN FOUR divergent cases has the minority holding the correct answer, because shared pretraining correlates LLM errors and breaks the independence the Condorcet Jury Theorem assumes. leave() re-evaluating an open proposal (hub.ts:407) while voters() drops the departed (hub.ts:422-423) is that suppression implemented in code. We take their finding and NOT their mechanism: a LightGBM classifier that decides when to overturn a majority is a larger change than this list and sits badly with consensus-requires-scrutiny, which wants a legible reason rather than a model's confidence. No RE-TARGET: none of the three reverses anything here.
    
    HOW TO VERIFY. (1) In a 2-agent room propose, have the other disagree, then amend — amend must succeed and the notice must name amend. (2) Conclude a room: the conclusion message must be under 200 chars and room.conclusion must still hold the text. (3) Call wait twice with no amend between: the second must omit the proposal text and name the version. (4) Send without force with unread present, twice: the second must not repeat the batch. (5) Pass after a withheld human message, then wait: the message must still arrive. (6) Amend, then check the amender shows as not-yet-voted. (7) Have the second-to-last agreer call leave_room: it must be refused once with the proposal id.
```
